# Follow-up import experiments

Proposed 2026-09-24, after the independently verified results in
[processing experiments](processing-experiments.md). These are experiment designs, not new
measurements, implementation approvals or changes to completed experiment results. The
[application work list](../todo/readme.md) remains the status authority. F1–F6 below are local
proposal labels, not new approved T/CRS task IDs. No experiments in this document have run.

**Execution update, 2026-09-25:** the opening proposal is preserved as written. The owner
subsequently authorized execution; measured results and independent verifications are appended
below under [execution authorization and results](#execution-authorization-and-results).
F1 and the F4/F5 extensions are verified; live quality and OCR/integration work continue.
A positive review of the original design is separate from the still-pending execution review.

## What the results suggest

The two-page run did not fail an extraction-quality test. It reached an arbitrary cumulative
16-session allowance. A fresh rerun reproduced its first 1,024 measurement rows exactly, then
one explicit Resume completed all 800 pages: 1,222 requests and 987,624,881 serialized text
characters. Ten-page units completed the same scripted workload in 896 requests and
393,189,513 characters. Both recordings remain valid and separately documented in
[T1/T4](processing-experiments.md#t1-and-t4-where-the-input-characters-go).

**Fifteen and twenty pages are worth testing, but “larger is better” is not established.**
For the existing scripted schedule, each page still needs its own read. Larger units reduce
publication calls, not those reads. The schedule model is `C = N + ceil(N/U) + 2`,
`S = ceil(C/63)`, `R = C + S`, where C counts executed tool calls, S sessions and R requests.
For 800 pages the model predicts:

- U2: 1,222 requests; U5: 978; U10: 896.
- U15: 870, only 26 fewer than U10 (2.90%).
- U20: 856, only 40 fewer than U10 (4.46%).
- U30: 843; U50: 831, 7.25% fewer than U10.

Only U2 and U10 above are completed measurements; the others are predictions. The current
[plan contract](../../src/server/intake-plan.ts) permits U1–50. Character volume could improve
more than request count, as in U2 versus U10, but could also worsen. The current character model
already misses its per-request criterion; do not invent residual-growth parameters for U15/U20.

Bigger units also delay the first proposal, enlarge a failed publication's retry scope, and
require facts from more pages to survive before publication. The fake model remembered facts
in a JavaScript closure; a real model faces evidence eviction, output limits and association
errors. Therefore the next decision is the **smallest efficient unit that preserves quality**,
not the largest permitted number.

For this particular schedule the first publication takes `U + 3` requests: 13 at U10,
18 at U15 and 23 at U20. These are modeled request counts, not measured waiting times. That
trade-off matters even when the larger unit reduces total job requests.

OCR and fingerprint failures suggest specific causes worth isolating. T18 lost paragraphs
on skewed pages and inserted a footer between columns; T12 matched a changed value and lost
all matches under row-major column extraction. Sweeping more parameters without separating
those causes would give weak evidence.

## Common protocol

- Follow the [runner/verifier rules](processing-experiments.md#rules-for-runners-and-verifiers):
  fictional inputs, isolated prototype worktrees, raw artifacts outside Git, immutable prior
  results, explicit measured/model/unknown labels and independent verification. Proposals here
  add endpoints; they do not lower the original T12, T18 or stage-4 criteria.
- Preserve failed historical fixtures as regression cases. Build development and held-out
  sets from different document templates and seeds, not different corruptions of the same
  underlying document. Freeze code, parameters, manifests, hashes and acceptance rules before
  opening held-out scores. A failed holdout becomes regression evidence; further tuning needs
  a newly reserved holdout, never repeated claims against the same test set.
- Put literal expected facts, dates, reading order, regions and edit locations in a separate
  scoring oracle. Runtime algorithms receive only the ordinary source inputs. No hidden
  generator IDs, known skew angles, oracle encounter boundaries or expected strings may guide
  candidate selection, preprocessing or retries.
- Log errors, abstentions, incomplete coverage and timeouts alongside successes. Report raw
  numerators/denominators by document and failure family. Zero errors in a small corpus is a
  bounded observation, not a universal guarantee.
- Unit-size comparisons must finish the same workload. Preserve the initial-allowance stop
  and full-run totals separately. For scripted F1 runs, permit the same existing explicit
  Resume mechanism when monotonic progress and remaining work are present; record every
  extension. Never discard an expensive arm because it hits the 16-session allowance.
  Unexpected non-progress/failure stays a finding. This does not change production limits.
- F2 is a proposed live stage-4 extension, still unexecuted. Resolve T20's HTTP 400 first,
  verify the chosen route and record reported usage, predeclare fixture × strategy × repeats
  and token estimates, and follow rule 8's 2× stop rule. A stopped arm is incomplete, never a
  cheap success. No automatic extension overrides that live experiment stop rule; inability
  to complete fairly leaves the comparison inconclusive. The missing historical 31 requests
  are not required: collect fresh request durations and usage for these fictional runs.

## F1 — Find the useful unit-size range

**Question:** do U15/U20 materially reduce repeated input after U10, and where do returns flatten?
Extends T1/T4 and informs CRS-086 and T23; does not select a production default.

**Design:** first run the deterministic dense N200 and N800 fixtures at U2, 5, 10, 15, 20, 30
and 50: 14 completed cells. Fix one-page reads, zero overlap, record strings, publication per
unit, transcript eviction and 64-round sessions. Use the same checkout/environment for all
new cells; preserve old results as reference rather than pooling their local wall times.
One run per cell suffices for deterministic counts. Do not claim a timing speedup from these
single runs. If counts differ on repetition, explain the difference before comparison.

Before the matrix, validate the generator with N23/U15 and N23/U20: final units contain eight
and three pages respectively, with every page proposed exactly once. Include the N800/U15
five-page tail. Adapt publication to actual unit membership, not an assumed full U-sized tail.
Do not send the scripted model whole-document facts as a substitute for normal reads.

**Measure:** complete page/record/unit coverage; per-request and cumulative text characters;
peak request text/media; request/session/publication counts; fresh-session seed versus current
evidence versus retained history, using disjoint accounting where possible; observed extensions;
proposal bytes/records; pages and requests before first proposal. Unresolved exact history
attribution stays unknown. Measure duplicate/missing page reads and retained-original hash.
Provider tokens, cache savings and real-model quality remain unknown in this scripted test.

**Decision rule, proposed before execution:** no candidate advances with missing/duplicate
coverage or guard violations. An additional 10% reduction in total serialized text versus U10
is the proposed threshold for an efficiency lead; report smaller gains too. Keep all complete
arms on a trade-off plot of total characters against requests-to-first-proposal and peak payload.
Do not eliminate U15/U20 from F2 solely for missing this exploratory 10% threshold: quality or
first-review behavior could still decide between them. U30/U50 are useful probes of diminishing
returns, not presumptive winners. A result where requests fall but text or first-review delay
rises is informative.

**Next decision:** carry U2, U10, U15 and U20 into the real-quality comparison. If U30/U50 shows
a distinct advantage, declare a separate live arm before any of its live results are observed.

## F2 — Check whether larger units retain facts and become useful sooner

**Question:** which of U2/U10/U15/U20 preserves extraction quality at the best measured cost?
Extends T6/T7/T8; informs T23 rather than duplicating a model-chosen-plan implementation.

**Design:** use four fixed sizes, three fictional families, two held-out documents per family,
and five repeats: 120 runs, paired by document/repeat with a predeclared balanced arm order.
Families are a dense 40-page multi-record chart, a 40-page mixed-density chart with encounters
crossing unit boundaries, and a 200-page single report with identity/headings early and findings
late. Author literal facts and source locators, including negation, units, corrections and
cross-page associations. Each family has different templates in development and evaluation.
Rotate fact locations relative to U10/U15/U20 boundaries; keep the same actual files across arms.
Development fixtures validate the oracle; the six held-out documents are not tuning material.

Hold route/model, prompts, media policy, caching setting and context retention constant. Record
actual upstream cache counts and order effects. Do not combine larger units with a new working
summary or different schema in this comparison. If current eviction loses early facts, that is
a finding; test the remedy in a separately declared arm. Explicit Resume is permitted only
within the declared live run/stop policy, with its cost and intervention recorded.

**Measure:** literal record recall/precision, duplicates, correct person/date/value/unit links,
cross-page association recall and valid source locators; reported input/cached/output tokens;
requests and errors/retries; complete-job time; time to first valid reviewable record and to
50% of expected records. Preserve every failed/partial attempt. Report first-review latency
separately from total completion time; larger batches might improve one and worsen the other.

**Quality gate:** preserve the T6/T7 margins: recall and association recall no more than two
percentage points below U2, precision no more than one point below, duplicates no higher.
Additionally report comparison against U10 and every family separately; no newly wrong-person
association or unsupported critical value may be hidden in the aggregate. Use paired 95%
intervals, resampling whole documents with their repeats together rather than treating facts
as independent. Six document clusters may be insufficient for narrow intervals: a bound that
crosses a margin means **inconclusive**, even if its point estimate passes. A predeclared new
document cohort is then needed; repeating the same six files does not create more document
diversity. Lock the scoring protocol before execution.

**Decision:** among sizes meeting the quality gate, retain the trade-off between total tokens,
first-review time and total completion time. Prefer the smaller size when gains are uncertain;
do not infer provider time from scripted request count or character volume. Report measured
latency separately from any exploratory regression on fresh request-level usage.

## F3 — Repair OCR geometry before replacing the engine

**Question:** were T18's failures mainly skew and reading order, and can host preprocessing fix
them within the original CPU target? Extends T18/CRS-100; adoption remains a separate decision.

**Design:** keep T18's pinned Tesseract CLI, English traineddata, 300 dpi and PSM 3 for a 2×2
ablation: baseline; automatic deskew only; automatic column/block reconstruction only; both.
Deskew estimates angles from the image. Reconstruction uses OCR word boxes and detected regions,
not oracle boxes; it must retain footer/side-note content and must not treat table cells as
ordinary independent columns. Keep recognized words fixed when isolating the ordering effect.
This first experiment does not also change DPI, traineddata, engine or threshold.

Use the original 300-dpi stress cases as regressions, plus 60 development and 60 held-out pages:
three layout families (tables, columns, annotated reports) × four independent documents × five
conditions (clean, positive skew, negative skew, perspective, blur/compression). The held-out
set uses new template families, values, fonts and document seeds. Fix distortion parameters in
the manifest before running; the algorithm never receives them. Use realistic dense fictional
paragraphs and tables. Handwritten-font cases remain explicitly simulated; natural handwriting
is not qualified by this test.

Run all four frozen arms on the held-out set. Any selection occurs on development only. Record
every paragraph omission and reading-order error alongside the original sequence-sensitive CER;
an auxiliary order-insensitive score cannot replace CER. Count exact critical value/unit/date/
negation retention, since a low average CER can conceal a changed digit.

**Criteria:** keep CER ≤3% and total CPU ≤5 seconds/page on one core, including deskew and
reconstruction. Report the original aggregate and every layout/distortion stratum separately;
a failing stratum prevents a broad quality claim. Report page maxima and counts exceeding
each threshold. Use three CPU repeats per held-out arm/page, with fixed resource limits and
rotated order; report median and maximum rather than selecting the best repeat. Keep cold-start
and warm-worker timings separate, count both for deployment decisions, and record process peak
RSS, image/runtime sizes and preprocessing overhead. No critical-field loss introduced versus
the baseline on the same page is acceptable without a recorded unresolved failure.

If geometry repair fails, a later isolated comparison can test region-specific PSM 6 or another
traineddata set, holding the chosen preprocessing fixed. Do not select the best mode per page
using the answer key. The causal motivation is supported by Tesseract's own documentation on
[skew and segmentation](https://tesseract-ocr.github.io/tessdoc/ImproveQuality.html); success on
our corpus remains an experiment, not a promised consequence of that guidance.

## F4 — Separate “related passage” from “unchanged content”

**Question:** can fingerprints find the right old passage without hiding a small correction?
Extends T12/CRS-095; fingerprints may label/order work, never skip processing.

**Design:** compare current similarity-only classification against fingerprint candidate
retrieval followed by aligned literal token differences. For this experiment use unambiguous
single-column text PDFs so layout is not another moving variable. Candidate regions come from
host-derived paragraphs/windows and real text coordinates; no STARTSPAN markers or oracle
encounter intervals. Keep the current k=10/w=6/0.8 baseline and boilerplate discounting fixed
for the initial contrast. Oracle alignment scores output but never provides runtime boundaries.

Use separate development and held-out corpora, each with 100 baseline fictional encounters
across ten independently authored template families. For each encounter make one genuine
unchanged re-render, one changed version and one unrelated same-template distractor: 300 queries
per split against that split's 100-reference library. Spread the 100 changes evenly over value,
unit, date, negation and deleted-fact edits. Include decimal/sign changes and repeated boilerplate;
randomize edit positions so changes do not always appear beside distinctive identifiers.
Remove generator IDs from runtime text. Fix which simple rendering variations apply to each
query in the manifest. Add the old 5.4→5.7 case as regression, not fresh test evidence.

Both arms emit a candidate identity and one of: unchanged supported text, related but changed,
or uncertain. Similarity alone may nominate a candidate; the new arm must account for all
content-bearing tokens before declaring unchanged. Normalization may fold Unicode form and
whitespace, but must preserve digits, signs, decimal points, dates, units and negation; no broad
header removal may erase an edit. Any unaligned or ambiguous region prevents unchanged status.
Even an equal text layer is a statement about that text layer, not proof of identical visuals.

**Criteria:** retain T12 unchanged precision ≥0.99 and recall ≥0.95, with zero planted changes
labeled unchanged. Define true positives as a correct-reference unchanged decision on an
unchanged query; a changed/unrelated/wrong-reference unchanged decision is a false positive;
a missed/uncertain/wrong-reference unchanged query is a false negative. Undefined precision
cannot pass. Score candidate identity top-1/top-5 retrieval separately for changed and unchanged
queries, plus edit-localization recall, unrelated-candidate rate and abstention/coverage. An
always-uncertain system fails unchanged recall. Report per-edit-family outcomes and intervals;
100 changed cases with zero errors still do not prove a population error rate below 1%.

## F5 — Recover matches across layouts without an oracle

**Question:** does layout-aware segmentation fix T12's row-major column failure without
introducing incorrect associations? Keep this separate from F4's correction-detection test.

**Design:** cross two factors: raw extraction order versus host-inferred block/column order,
and similarity-only versus F4's candidate-plus-change check. Use the old row-major case as a
regression. New development and held-out sets each contain 20 fictional documents spanning
five distinct template families; each document has four encounter blocks. Render each as
column-major, row-major, re-paginated and mixed-table/sidebar forms, with one content correction
and one same-template distractor per document. Keep the reference content identical across arms.
Holdout templates and text differ from development. Infer blocks from ordinary PDF text-item
coordinates; the current joined-string identity reader may need an isolated experimental
geometry export from [the PDF worker](../../src/server/intake-pdf-worker.ts).

Run full candidate discovery, boilerplate discounting, retrieval and change classification.
No oracle spans, known block count or manually fixed column positions may be runtime inputs.
Ground-truth regions are scoring-only. Missing and extra blocks count as segmentation errors,
not exclusions. Use one-to-one predicted/true encounter matching for scoring; a wrong-reference
match counts as both a false positive and a missed true match, and duplicate predictions count
as false positives. Also score coverage by literal fact tokens and source locators so fragmenting
one encounter into many tiny correct fragments cannot inflate recall.

**Criteria:** T12's ≥0.95 unchanged recall, ≥0.99 precision and zero false-unchanged corrections
must hold overall and for each layout. Report segmentation precision/recall, candidate identity,
unmatched content, abstentions, CPU and peak memory. If order recovery succeeds but change
detection fails, report those as different results; do not accept the combined pipeline.

## F6 — Check the whole OCR-to-index pipeline and its uncertainty

**Question:** do the improvements remain useful when OCR errors and automatic matching interact?
This replaces T18's limited known-span-containment diagnostic with a full downstream test.

**Prerequisites and design:** freeze the selected F3 preprocessing and F4/F5 matching before
opening a new held-out set of 20 fictional document pairs from five unseen template families,
four pages per document. Each pair has a baseline export and a re-export containing a correction
and new content. The scorer holds literal spans, edit locations and expected date roles. Runtime
sees only source PDFs/images and the earlier import's permitted index. Cross baseline/improved
OCR with baseline/improved automatic matching (four arms on the same pairs); include a clean
text-layer control to distinguish OCR damage from matching failure. Log every stage's failures.

**Measure and gates:** for eligible text-layer cases, full-pipeline unchanged precision/recall
with F4/F5's definitions and T12's thresholds; for all cases, candidate identity,
new-content retrieval, critical edit recovery, and T13
date-role retention with zero false exclusions in an offline scorer. No product date skipping
is introduced. Report T18's ≥0.9 recovered-span recall separately; it cannot substitute for
precision or a full matcher test. Count OCR omissions as downstream misses, even when the
matcher itself ran correctly. Equal OCR output must never certify unchanged original content:
an image-derived match remains related/uncertain unless independently supported by the original.

Test a quality/uncertainty signal on a separate development split with the same corpus structure,
then freeze it. Candidate signals can include OCR confidence, unassigned image regions and
layout disagreement; none is assumed reliable. Report the held-out risk-versus-coverage curve,
false-confident documents and abstained share. Proposed useful gate: at least 80% of documents
receive a usable related-passage index, zero accepted indexes hide a planted critical correction,
and unchanged decisions on eligible text-layer cases still meet T12's precision/recall bars.
For images, abstaining from exact sameness is correct behavior, not an excuse to omit related
candidate recall. Report image-derived and text-layer outcomes separately. Twenty pairs provide
screening evidence only; zero observed critical misses is not a deployment safety guarantee.

Re-exports are always read in full. Unknown or poor OCR stays visible and available to the
existing reading path. A quality signal may qualify an index, never suppress an original page.
If F3–F5 fail, this integration test is deferred; retain their failure evidence first.

## Recommended order and what not to conclude yet

Start with **F1**, which directly answers whether 15/20 pages improve the measured workload,
and **F3/F4**, which isolate the known OCR and corrected-value failures. Next run F5 to remove
the earlier oracle/layout limitation. F2 is the necessary live quality check before recommending
a unit default; F6 tests the combined index only after its components justify it. F1 can proceed
without historical diagnostics; F2 needs a working route, not that old file.

A further targeted extension of T21 would test patient-label-to-DOB association across table
rows and line breaks: those were its two missed wrong-person cases. Retain the same zero-false-stop
criterion, add relatives/subscribers/guarantors as negative controls, and measure improvement on
new fictional templates before combining it with OCR. This is lower priority than answering
the unit-size and OCR/fingerprint questions above.

The first useful result may be “U10 is already close to the request-count knee,” “deskew fixes
omissions but ordering still fails,” or “fingerprints are good retrieval hints but cannot certify
unchanged content.” Each narrows a real design choice. None justifies automatic skipping, a
production default change or a claim of clinical extraction accuracy from scripted transport.

## Independent design review

Independent verifier subagent, 2026-09-24: reviewed the owner's original request for useful
follow-ups, including U15/U20, OCR and fingerprinting, and this written proposal against the
verified T1/T4, T12 and T18 evidence and current unit-size contract.

**Positive score: 9/10 overall; no material design blocker.** Prompt coverage 10/10; grounding
9/10; executable design 9/10; fair controls/criteria 9/10; usefulness/prioritization 9/10.
The reviewer independently checked the request predictions, supported U1–50 range,
nondivisible-unit hazard and original failure scopes. The review found that the sequence
answers the 15/20-page question, distinguishes complete scripted cost from live quality, and
tests OCR geometry, changed-content detection and automatic layout matching with controlled
arms and reserved holdouts, while preserving full reading and prior criteria.

Two optional clarity improvements were incorporated: explicit first-publication request
counts and explicit restriction of F6's unchanged-content criteria to eligible text-layer
cases. Image-derived matches remain related/uncertain. This is a review of the design's
usefulness and rigor, not authorization to run live experiments or evidence that an unrun arm
will pass. Earlier experiment results and their independent verifications remain unchanged.

## Execution authorization and results

### Run plan 2026-09-24 — coordinator

The owner subsequently authorized pursuing all of these tests through independent review for
completeness and breadth. This supersedes only the earlier unexecuted/unapproved status above;
the proposed methods, original criteria and historical design review are preserved. Isolated
F1–F6 worktrees were created using the owner's existing worktree authorization. All remain at
baseline `7dc23908299f5e79a0a324713d717f5df95f2778`; prototypes and raw fictional evidence stay
outside the main checkout. No production change, commit, merge or push is part of this work.

F1 unit sizes, F3 OCR, and F4/F5 fingerprints run with separate agents. The coordinator prepares
F2's route and live harness. Every result requires a different verifier; the final completeness
score is independent of whether any hypothesis passes. F6 retains its prerequisite gate unless
a separately recorded diagnostic extension is declared before running it.

F2 prerequisite plan: reproduce the prior single fictional transport probe with diagnostic
error classification, then at most three additional one-request probes changing only the
identified compatibility field or testing a minimal request/tool exchange. Each request's
serialized content and input/4 + requested-output token model are saved before dispatch, with
the 2× threshold checked against returned usage. A missing/stripped provider output cap is
explicitly unknown and a post-response check cannot guarantee an in-flight token bound.
No owner archive is mounted or profile opened. Existing operator containers stay stopped;
only an isolated proxy copy uses the configured route and existing authentication. These small
prerequisite probes do not count as the F2 extraction matrix or route acceptance.

### Route prerequisite observation and next plan 2026-09-24 — coordinator

The four planned probes are retained separately. Probe 1 reproduced HTTP 400 and identified
unsupported `reasoning_effort`; probes 2 and 4 without that option returned HTTP 400, with the
fourth retaining the specific upstream reason: `System messages are not allowed`. Probe 3,
a minimal user-only request, returned HTTP 200 and the requested `READY`: 1,644 input plus
5 output tokens, including 1,624 tokens attributed to adapter instructions. This exceeded
its predeclared 2× estimate and that single-request run ended; it is a calibration finding.
Probe 4's new plan included the observed 1,624-token prefix estimate before dispatch.

The unsuccessful probes used an array-valued system message with a cache marker. Current
application code with caching off sends its system instructions as a string and never sends
`reasoning_effort`. Before any extraction run, declare two additional one-request transport
checks: convert only that system content to its plain string (same text), then, if it succeeds,
check a simple function-call response using that accepted request shape. Each is one fictional
page × one strategy × one repeat; its new plan includes the measured adapter-prefix estimate
and the same 2× rule. This extends prerequisite diagnosis, not any earlier result or criterion.

### Execution clarification 2026-09-24 — F6 diagnostic extension

The owner's request to pursue all tests includes measuring end-to-end behavior even if a
component misses its acceptance criteria. Preserve the original F6 adoption prerequisite and
its failure/deferred outcome. After recording F3–F5 results, additionally run the same frozen
F6 corpus, four arms and clean control as a **diagnostic error-propagation experiment**, using
the predeclared development-selected variants. Keep all original acceptance bars and held-out
separation. An unsuccessful component is an experimental variant, not an accepted improvement;
this extension cannot authorize an index, skipping or a product change. Its concrete manifest
and checks must be saved before the first F6 run.

## F4 results — separate retrieval from exact unchanged text

### Run 2026-09-25 — fingerprint runner, primary frozen comparison

- Environment: checkout `7dc23908299f5e79a0a324713d717f5df95f2778`, Node `v24.19.0`, macOS arm64, PDF.js `6.3.289`, offline fictional PDFs. Prototype only in the authorized `f4-fingerprints` worktree. No product edits, profile reads, live requests, accepted writes or Git mutations.
- Corpus: measured 100 reference encounters and 300 queries **per split**, ten separately authored narrative template families per split, with distinct development/held-out templates and seeds. Each source has an unchanged re-render, a changed version and a same-template unrelated distractor. The 100 changes distribute 20 each over signed/decimal values, units, dates, negation and a deleted sentence. Runtime text has no generator IDs or boundary markers. The separate manifest contains expected text/edits. Seed is `42139 + 139*family + 491*case` for development and `83017 + 139*family + 491*case` for heldout; the narrative choice LCG is `state = (1664525*state + 1013904223) mod 2^32`. The source generator records all twenty literal templates and ten narrative clauses.
- Rendering: actual single-column PDF files, reference Helvetica; queries cycle ordinary Helvetica/Courier/Times-Roman, different margins, line widths and sizes. Actual PDF.js `getTextContent()` extracts text boxes. Primary segmentation groups observed x anchors within 12 points and breaks at gaps greater than 1.7 times median item height. Neither expected strings, block counts nor oracle regions are runtime inputs.
- Both arms retain k=10, w=6, query-fingerprint containment threshold 0.8, SHA-256 shingles/rightmost-min winnowing, discount fingerprints present in at least four indexed reference regions. The second arm also compares all NFKC-normalized literal tokens using LCS differences, preserving signs, decimals, dates, units, punctuation and negation. Tied candidates abstain. No source content is stripped as a header. Reference IDs identify source files, not hidden text identifiers.
- Freeze: primary algorithm/fixtures/runners recorded at `2026-09-25T03:56:54.529Z`, before heldout execution. SHA-256 core `a3c710f1c3af7471edfcb4afeb1270c1432173a391fff0718f7ffd8f5f8af2d1`; generator `933255e1070617e9fdd70aa871ccd9504a6cbc62d70d563742c5f611de1c6f85`; runner `bae56ff2064629f7398bfad0e4e75bfe6a87ba90d51e61fd845ada389aee8271`. Generator seeds and rendering schedule were frozen before opening scores; each run writes its literal manifest before generating and scoring files.
- Commands, from that prototype: `EXPERIMENT_ARTIFACTS=<external>/f4 node src/scripts/run-f4.ts development`, then `EXPERIMENT_ARTIFACTS=<external>/f4 node src/scripts/run-f4.ts heldout`. Receipts: `development-result.json`, `heldout-result.json`, per-split manifests, and all 800 PDFs with SHA-256 in the result inventory. Local external receipt root is `<external>/f4`.
- **Measured primary heldout:** similarity-only TP 60/FP 21/FN 40, precision 60/81 = 0.740741, recall 60/100 = 0.60, with 21/100 planted changes called unchanged. Literal check TP 60/FP 0/FN 40, precision 1, recall 0.60, false-unchanged changes0/100. Both arms therefore **fail** the original bars: similarity fails precision and recall; literal check fails recall. Development had the same total counts; it is recorded separately.
- Measured candidate top-1/top-5 identity is61/100 and 62/100 for each of unchanged and changed queries. Only 21 changed queries reached the 0.8 threshold;79 abstained. All100 unrelated queries were below threshold. A top-ranked identity below threshold is not an accepted candidate. Literal-change localization over changed cases: value 1/40 edited-token positions, unit 40/40, date 118/118, negation 0/60, deleted-fact 120/120. These conditional failures are not concealed by zero false-unchanged outcomes. Every heldout template family recovered6/10 unchanged queries.
- Diagnosis: the 40 failed unchanged re-renders use Courier. PDF.js splits many ordinary lines into multiple text items; primary x-anchor clustering incorrectly interprets these as separate columns. These queries produced27–44 regions each, rather than one. This is an automatic-segmentation failure, **not** evidence that repeated boilerplate alone defeated retrieval. Earlier informal suspicion of boilerplate sparsity was disproven by the region receipts.
- Descriptive Wilson95% intervals: primary literal recall 0.5020–0.6906 and precision 0.9398–1. These are encounter-level descriptive intervals, not a guarantee across template populations. Zero errors in 100 changed cases has a one-sided exact95% upper bound about2.95%; it does not establish less than 1% population error.
- Oddity: an initial setup attempt called `PDFDocumentProxy.destroy`, which the installed API does not provide; it stopped before a scored corpus. Corrected to `PDFDocumentLoadingTask.destroy`, and TypeScript errors were fixed before the freeze. The failed setup was not counted as a scored arm.

### Run 2026-09-25 — fingerprint runner, separately frozen line-reconstruction extension

- The primary failure above remains unchanged. A new, declared extension joins text items on the same observed baseline within 0.2×item height and horizontal gaps≤2×maximum adjacent height, then invokes the unchanged primary segmenter/index/classifier. It uses no font names or expected strings. This is an additional arm, not a corrected primary result.
- Reserved **new heldout**: ten newly authored narrative families,100 reference/300 query PDFs, seed `126751 + 139*family + 491*case`, the same five edits20 each and rendering schedule. An additional development/regression cohort uses original development template families with seed159019. There was no tuning against the new holdout; the candidate was fixed before all extension scoring. The later development receipt is a regression check, not a selection claim.

## F5 results — actual geometry, automatic regions and continuity

### Run 2026-09-25 — fingerprint runner, primary 2×2 layout comparison

- Same environment and frozen core as F4. Worktree `f5-layout`, source `src/scripts/run-f5.ts` SHA-256 `11e8c80c0811c049dd7903ecf385c036ea752a8960334821bf72276e441c58d2`. Twenty documents/five template families **per split**, four source encounter blocks/document. Heldout families and seeds differ from development. Every document has two unchanged blocks, one corrected block and one same-template distractor. References are the four earlier source encounters for that document, identical across arms; this is a per-document index comparison, not a100-reference library search.
- Four actual PDF variants per document: column-major, row-major, repaginated (one encounter crosses two pages) and mixed-table/sidebar. Column x coordinates vary with document number. Runtime infers anchors; it receives neither their intended coordinates nor the expected block count. The mixed variant also includes a two-cell footer; its literal text is retained and scored as an additional non-encounter region, so total scoring regions340 =320 encounter blocks +20 footers per split. This additional content cannot disappear silently.
- Cross raw extraction-order segmentation versus inferred geometry, and similarity-only versus literal check. Predicted/true regions are matched one-to-one using scoring-only source coordinates: at least95% predicted-item and 95% truth-line coverage; duplicates/extra fragments are false positives. Unchanged TP requires a complete matched true encounter and its correct reference ID. Literal-token/source-locator coverage is reported, not just fragment-level retrieval.
- Commands: `EXPERIMENT_ARTIFACTS=<external>/f5 node src/scripts/run-f5.ts development`, then `heldout`. Receipts `<external>/f5/{development-result.json,heldout-result.json}` include100 actual PDFs/split, file hashes, every region/classification, document/family/layout scores, CPU and memory.
- **Measured heldout all layouts:** raw+similarity TP 40/FP 132/FN 120, precision 0.232558, recall 0.25; raw+literal40/0/120, precision 1, recall 0.25; inferred+similarity120/170/40, precision 0.413793, recall 0.75; inferred+literal120/0/40, precision 1, recall 0.75. False-unchanged corrected-region predictions52,0,90,0 respectively. These are region-prediction counts, which may exceed the 80 distinct changed blocks when fragmented; they are not presented as percentages of unique corrections.
- Inferred+literal per-layout recall: column-major40/40, row-major40/40, mixed/sidebar40/40, repaginated0/40 (precision undefined there, hence cannot pass). It fixes the new row-major encounter-block layout but cannot join page fragments. **Overall fails original0.95 recall.** Development inferred+literal has the same 120/0/40 counts, separately recorded.
- Measured inferred segmentation:240/340 true regions matched,200 extra predicted regions among 440 predictions; full-region literal fact coverage 33033/44164 tokens;946/6141 source items unmatched,5195/6141 mapped into complete regions. Correct-reference candidate identities 180/240 eligible related blocks.150/440 region predictions uncertain. Raw segmentation 80/340 matched,3690 extras, literal coverage 11011/44164. This explicitly penalizes fragmentation.
- Measured process CPU (generation/extraction/indexing/all four arms together)1.286854 seconds heldout; process peak RSS250592 KiB. These are single host-run totals, not per-arm speedup evidence or a constrained-core performance qualification.

### Run 2026-09-25 — fingerprint runner, separately frozen page-continuation extension

- Preserve the primary75% recall failure. Additional runtime change: start with F4v2 line reconstruction, then consider ordinary consecutive-page regions. Keep an already uniquely aligned region; otherwise concatenate adjacent-page regions until a unique prior-index reference reaches literal-token alignment≥0.9, stopping if combined length exceeds1.2×maximum reference length. Alignment is `1 - inserted/deleted token count / max(reference,query token count)`; equality ambiguity abstains. No truth boundaries, expected block count, known layout family or oracle IDs enter the algorithm. The same source/reconstruction is used for similarity-only and literal arms.
- New holdout:20 documents from **five newly authored, unseen templates**, seed base219941. Same four layout variants plus different-encounter adjacency and critical-field-line boundary controls:120 PDF variants, each with four encounter blocks. References20 PDFs, total140 files. A separate development/regression cohort uses seed197887. New holdout was frozen before scoring; later development is a regression check, not model selection. Boundary controls place a break beside a printed critical-field line; they do not establish every possible token-level correction/boundary combination.
- Freeze `2026-09-25T04:03:32.580Z`: continuation module SHA-256 `fbc9ad0d9e267f84093b1e2b8d7c1d19edc280ac75733302c69a02e54d0a9e48`; generator `0ec3357d05df82dc9c904d14899c32d896e24785c2fb42e177b0e083250d362a`; runner `12f85df9bb915b1be65cb887296671d5d582458c5965832604cd56e888c078f4`, plus the frozen primary/v2 hashes above.

Correct related-reference candidate identities 360/360 complete related blocks (240 unchanged,120 changed). The original unchanged precision/recall/change bars pass for this reserved corpus. Development also 240/0/0. No wrong full-encounter merge was scored in the different-encounter adjacency control.
- **Limit remains:** segmentation 440/500 scoring regions matched,120 extra predictions among 560 total, token coverage 61346/67434, source locator coverage 7656/8216 items,560 unmatched items,206 uncertain region predictions. New/unrelated content split across pages has no supporting prior reference and therefore remains fragmented; mixed-table footer cells remain split. Per repaginated/boundary variant,60/80 full encounter blocks matched: the 20 unrelated blocks remain fragmented, while unchanged and corrected blocks are recovered. ### Historical regressions and verification handoff

- All eight earlier T12 fixture families were retained as regression evidence: baseline, font/margin/pagination, header/footer, appended year, reordering, corrected5.4→5.7, column-major within an encounter and row-major within an encounter. To remove the earlier oracle dependency, authoring converts the historical PDFs into ordinary PDFs with only the explicit STARTSPAN/ENDSPAN marker text items removed; content/coordinates/headers/footers remain. The runtime then extracts those new PDFs and never receives marker strings or expected spans. The scoring oracle alone uses the original literal encounter text. This is explicitly a marker-free derivative regression, not a claim of rerunning the original oracle-assisted algorithm unchanged.
- Commands from F4 prototype: `node src/scripts/run-fingerprint-regressions.ts` and `node src/scripts/run-fingerprint-regressions-v2.ts`; external receipts `<external>/fingerprint-regressions/result.json` and `<external>/fingerprint-regressions-v2/result.json`. Each records8 variants×4 arms, all predictions, regenerated files. The regression script imports `fixtureSpans` from the retained T12 prototype; the original T12 receipts/results are untouched.
- Primary inferred+literal:24/24 unchanged on baseline, headers, appended year, reorder;23/23 on corrected value with zero false-unchanged correction. Font variant 0/24 and both within-encounter column variants0/24. V2 restores font variant 24/24 while preserving those other successes and zero false-unchanged corrected value. **Both historical within-encounter column variants still0/24, with undefined precision.** Their line spacing and split encounter geometry are outside this candidate's successful segmentation. The new F5 encounter-block results must not erase this historical failure. The continuation extension adds only consecutive-page joining; it does not claim a fix for these same-page failures.
- Focused checks: three literal/segmentation tests in F4 and three continuation/column-gutter tests in F5 pass. Full `npm run format:check` and `npm run typecheck` pass in each prototype after the final code changes. Tests preserve signed decimals, deletion/negation/unit changes, changed literals across a join, abstention for unrelated/nonadjacent fragments and actual column gutters. No runner self-verification is claimed; independent verification remains required.
- Overall decision: literal diffs are substantially safer than similarity-only unchanged labels on these fictional text layers. Ordinary word-box reconstruction and index-supported page continuation are useful repairs, but arbitrary column layout and segmentation of newly introduced content remain unresolved. No automatic skipping, production default or clinical correctness claim follows; F6 may proceed only as an explicitly labeled diagnostic extension while these failures remain.

## F2 results — live-route prerequisite

### Run 2026-09-25 — coordinator, compatibility and actual bridge checks

- Environment: baseline `7dc23908299f5e79a0a324713d717f5df95f2778`, Node
  `v24.19.0`, macOS arm64, the configured `health-primary` route resolving to
  `gpt-6-astra`. An isolated one-CPU proxy uses the existing configured image
  `sha256:b481cd8449c669793e4fe1a3c27f1d0e661a147f4173c366907b9558cd084125`
  and the current checkout's launcher. The original operator containers remain stopped.
  No health archive is mounted; authentication is used without recording its contents.
- Both additional predeclared compatibility probes succeeded. Probe 5 changed the previous
  array-valued system content to the same plain string: measured HTTP 200, 2,329 input plus
  9 output tokens, 2,338 total, 2,232 ms. Probe 6 requested a fictional function call and
  received the specified tool name and page argument: HTTP 200, 1,693 input plus 18 output
  tokens, 1,711 total, 2,122 ms. Neither exceeded its separately recorded 2× estimate.
  Earlier HTTP 400 receipts and the minimal probe's token-model overrun are preserved above.
- The actual application `testModelConnection` then ran its fictional text, image and PDF
  challenges, one repeat each, two requests per challenge. Predeclared model per challenge:
  `2 × (2,124 input + 128 output)` tokens, plus an explicitly uncalibrated 5,000-token
  media allowance for image/PDF. The stop threshold was twice that estimate, checked after
  each response and before any next request. All three challenges returned `readiness: tested`;
  measured totals were 3,745 tokens for text, 3,776 for image and 3,758 for PDF, with no
  overruns or failed requests. These checks validate the request path, not extraction quality.
- Interpretation: current application behavior with caching disabled uses string-valued
  system instructions and no `reasoning_effort` request field; that shape works on this route.
  The previous transport probe's unsupported parameter and array-valued system message explain
  its failures. This does not qualify the cache-enabled shape or establish provider output
  limits. Upstream automatic cached-token counts can still be nonzero with application
  prompt caching disabled; they must be measured in the matrix.
- Prototype commands: `node src/scripts/experiment-f2-route.ts start`, then
  `EXPERIMENT_PROXY_PREFIX_TOKENS=1624 node src/scripts/experiment-f2-route.ts probe <fictional-request.json>`
  for each of the two predeclared requests, and `node src/scripts/experiment-f2-preflight.ts`.
  External receipts retain each request's plan, response status/usage, challenge result and
  request duration. The extraction matrix will have its own frozen fixture and spend model;
  these prerequisite measurements are not counted as one of its 120 runs.
- Limitation: post-response token checks cannot guarantee an in-flight output bound. The
  adapter-prefix estimate came from the separately preserved minimal-request measurement;
  request framing and media may add usage. Full app acceptance gates remain unchanged.
  Independent verification is pending.

### Run 2026-09-25 — coordinator, matched native-PDF token calibration

Before freezing F2's run estimates, a separate development-only plan declared four single-request
probes: one dense and one sparse fictional page, each sent with and without its native PDF file.
The user prompt and `READY` response were identical. The two PDFs used development fonts,
identity, dates and values. This is token calibration, not a held-out extraction run.

Measured input was 1,650 tokens without either file, 1,828 with the dense file, and 1,724 with
the sparse file: additive deltas **178 and 74 input tokens per page**. All four returned five
output tokens and HTTP 200. Each reported 1,408 automatically cached input tokens, despite no
application cache markers. The predeclared selection was the larger measured delta, so F2's
model uses **178 media tokens per page exposure**, with the cost model's two exposures per page.
Generalization beyond these two text-layer PDF examples remains unvalidated; this does not
estimate scan/image costs or establish a provider limit.

Each calibration request had its own pre-dispatch estimate: non-media serialized characters/4
plus the observed 1,624-token adapter prefix and 128 output tokens; file arms additionally used
the earlier explicitly assumed 5,000-token media allowance. None exceeded its 2× threshold.
The new 178-token estimate is chosen before scored extraction and is never retroactively used
to relabel these calibration stops. Command: `node src/scripts/experiment-f2-media.ts` in the F2
prototype; external `media-calibration/plan.json`, per-request plans, `results.json` and
`summary.json` retain the paired evidence. Independent verification is pending.

## Independent verification — F4/F5 primary runs, extensions and historical regressions

### Verification 2026-09-25 — independent runner subagent

- Independence and method: this verifier did not author or run the F4/F5 candidate experiments. Read the briefs, frozen plans, generators, runtime core, continuation code and scoring code. Independently reconstructed prediction-level outcomes and aggregate statistics from both development and heldout receipts, checked all 2,080 recorded PDF hashes and the frozen source hashes, and reran reduced cases using the real retained PDFs. The verification scripts and receipts remain external: `circus-fingerprint-independent-verify.ts`, `circus-fingerprint-independent-historical.ts`, `verification.json` and `historical-rerun.json` under the verification artifact root. Execute the two scripts with Node v24.19.0, using the recorded prototype modules and external fixture roots; no original receipts are overwritten.
- Runtime boundary check: segmentation and classification consume actual PDF text items and the permitted prior-reference index. They do not consume truth regions, expected text, layout-family labels or a fixed encounter count. Authoring and scoring do use known fixture membership, as expected. Reference IDs are opaque source/region labels; they are not parsed as generator instructions. To check this distinction, the reduced reruns replaced every runtime reference ID with an arbitrary ordinal label before classification. All 200 reduced classifications reproduced statuses and the tested edit/score/locator results. No source marker or oracle identity was needed to recover the published outcomes.
- **Agree, F4 primary failure:** independently recovered similarity-only TP 60/FP 21/FN 40 (precision 60/81, recall 60/100), literal-check TP 60/FP 0/FN 40, and 21 versus zero planted false-unchanged decisions. Candidate top-1/top-5 counts and edit-family localization totals match. The diagnostic candidate counts use the first predicted region when segmentation yields multiple regions; they are not a ranking over a reconstructed whole encounter. The 40 missed unchanged queries are the Courier cases with fragmented regions. This retains the original recall failure.
- **Agree, F4 separately frozen extension:** new-heldout similarity-only TP 100/FP 26/FN 0 and literal-check TP 100/FP 0/FN 0 reproduce exactly. The literal arm reaches the bounded corpus precision/recall criteria, with zero of 100 planted changes called unchanged. Candidate identity is 100/100 at top-1 and top-5 for unchanged and changed queries; 26 changed queries reach the similarity threshold and 74 remain uncertain. The five localization totals 40/40, 40/40, 116/116, 60/60 and 120/120 agree. Development's separate outcomes also re-derived. This supports exact supported-text comparison on this corpus, not identical visuals, arbitrary layout handling or skipping pages.
- **Agree, F5 primary failure:** reconstructed all four arms from individual predictions. In order raw+similarity, raw+literal, inferred+similarity, inferred+literal, TP/FP/FN are 40/132/120, 40/0/120, 120/170/40 and 120/0/40. The inferred literal arm recovers 40/40 unchanged blocks in column-major, row-major and mixed/sidebar layouts, but 0/40 in repagination. Its precision is undefined on that failed layout, not a passing 1.0. Inferred segmentation is 240/340 matched true regions with 200 extra predictions among 440 predictions; token coverage 33,033/44,164 and locator coverage 5,195/6,141 agree.
- **Agree, F5 extension with an essential scope limit:** inferred+literal TP 240/FP 0/FN 0 and all six variants' 40/40 unchanged outcomes reproduce. Correct related-reference candidates are 360/360 complete related blocks. However, overall segmentation is only **440/500 = 88% recall and 440/560 = 78.571% precision**, with 120 extra predicted regions. Token coverage is 61,346/67,434 and locator coverage 7,656/8,216; 560 source items remain outside complete matched regions. The unrelated blocks split across pages and split footer cells remain failures. Thus the 1.0 unchanged-content scores are a narrow matching result, not a complete segmentation result or overall pipeline qualification. All eligible region matches in the actual receipts had full 1.0 item and truth-line coverage; the reported 95% eligibility threshold did not inflate these particular complete-region counts.
- **Agree, historical failures remain:** independently re-extracted every marker-free historical PDF and reran all eight variants × four arms × two cores, using arbitrary runtime reference IDs. All 64 rows reproduce their TP/FP/FN and statuses, and the extracted inputs contain no STARTSPAN/ENDSPAN markers. V2 restores the font/margin/pagination case to 24/24 unchanged, preserves the corrected-value case at 23/23 with zero false-unchanged correction, and preserves the other reported successes. Both within-encounter column variants remain 0/24 with undefined precision under both cores. These are failures even though the new encounter-block corpus scores well. The historical source/oracle is used only for scoring; the rerun runtime receives the unmarked PDFs and inferred regions.
- Arithmetic and artifacts: all reported aggregate counts, per-layout outcomes, partitions of region predictions, false-unchanged counts, candidate counts, CPU-receipt arithmetic and RSS units are consistent with the retained records. Single-run CPU/RSS values remain descriptive host measurements. The source and PDF hashes agree with the recorded freezes. No independent result discrepancy was found across the 53,712 primary/extension prediction records, 200 reduced classifications or 64 historical reruns.
- Decision: the reported bounded improvements and retained failures are independently supported. F4's literal-check extension passes its reserved text-layer corpus; F5's matched-content extension passes the stated unchanged-content metrics on its reserved corpus while document segmentation remains incomplete. Preserve these qualifications and the historical column failures. Neither a production adoption decision nor an OCR-to-index pipeline pass follows from this verification.

Retain numbered initial-stop and before/after Resume snapshots. Assert unchanged saved checkpoint, identity, earlier proposal IDs and cumulative counters, with the normal increment to extensions/budget offsets. Outer polling/test bounds remain 3,600/3,700 seconds; no production allowance is changed or bypassed.
- Measurement definitions: cumulative and peak text are serialized request characters excluding media strings. Media is measured as serialized media-string bytes, not provider tokens. Proposal sizes are actual retained JSONL bytes and parsed record counts. The disjoint structural partition is system, tools, first-request user-message positions (`seedUser`), other user messages, latest tool result, older tool results, assistant messages and framing. These sum exactly in every request. Latest results still include schema/snapshots and older results may remain useful evidence; chronology does not establish semantic staleness. Retained proposal bytes total 357,260, minimum/maximum 22,172/22,349; distinct proposal record counts 50. Text reduction versus same-N U10: 24.452%.

- Initial allowance versus full completion, **measured**: only N800/U2 needs an extension. It first stops at 1,024 requests / 16 slices / 830,087,567 characters, with 671 reads, 670 ready records, 335 accounted units, 65 remaining units and one pending window. The same saved run completes after one existing Resume, adding 198 requests / four slices / 157,537,314 characters. Numbered boundary and Resume receipts retain the initial stop separately. N800/U5 completes within 16 slices; all other arms require fewer slices. The N200/N800 U2 and U10 repetitions reproduce all four earlier complete character/request totals exactly; earlier wall times are not pooled.

- **Model versus measurement:** `C=N+ceil(N/U)+2; S=ceil(C/63); R=C+S` predicts the observed request/slice counts in all 16 cells, and `U+3` predicts every first-proposal request. The measured first-proposal page count is U. No residual-character or token model is invented for U5/U15/U20/U30/U50. The prior T1 calibration limitations remain.

- Against the frozen exploratory criterion: at N200, U15/U20 save **4.981%/7.570%** versus U10, below the proposed 10% text lead. U30/U50 save 10.628%/12.337%; U30 is the smallest tested size exceeding 10% there. At N800, U15/U20 save **15.261%/18.120%**; U15 is the smallest tested size exceeding 10%. U30/U50 save 24.467%/24.452%. These are efficiency leads for these scripted fixtures, not production defaults. All complete arms remain visible in `f1-tradeoffs.svg`: text characters versus first-proposal request, with circle area encoding peak request text. The chart uses separate labeled source-length axes and includes U10 as reference.

- Diminishing returns, **measured**: at N800, moving U30→U50 removes 12 requests but adds **59,208 text characters (0.020%)**, raises peak payload 613,121→616,911 characters and delays the first proposal 33→53 requests. The larger full proposal grows from at most 30 records /13,409 bytes to 50 records /22,349 bytes. At N200, U50 still lowers cumulative text versus U30, but peak payload rises 583,100→593,063 and first review is likewise delayed. This retains the retry/publication-size trade-off rather than declaring the largest unit a winner.

- Decision: retain U2/U10/U15/U20 for the already-designed F2 real-quality comparison. U30/U50 are useful probes whose live inclusion would need a separately predeclared arm; they are not added to F2 automatically. The corpus demonstrates complete scripted throughput with correct tail membership and a useful size-dependent efficiency range, while real extraction/association quality and provider token/latency effects remain unmeasured. Independent verification is required before this result is treated as closed.

## F7 — Patient-label and birth-date association follow-up

### Run plan 2026-09-25 — coordinator

The owner requested all proposed follow-ups, including the lower-priority T21 extension above.
A separate authorized `f7-identity` worktree compares the unchanged T21 rules with automatic
PDF row/cell reconstruction and bounded label-to-value association. This is an offline
text-layer experiment; it introduces no product gate, OCR identity decision or accepted write.
The original T21 recording remains unchanged.

Each development and held-out split has 100 wrong-DOB, 100 correct-person and 40 unknown files.
Cases cover next-line DOB values, separate-row demographic tables, inline and repeated headers,
and ambiguous patient/subscriber mixtures. Correct controls include subscribers, guarantors,
parents, emergency contacts, next of kin, conflicting cover/body identities and missing dates.
Unknown controls contain ambiguous numeric dates, invalid dates or unlabeled dates. Holdout
rendering changes labels, fonts, margins, item order and wrong-person dates. These are repeated
variations of a limited set of identity layouts, not 240 independent template families; report
unique source hashes as well as raw file counts. Retain the original 22 T21 PDFs as regressions
and recheck the original mixed-person ZIP per member.

Runtime receives ordinary Self metadata and actual extracted PDF text/coordinates. Ground-truth
labels and intended table columns are scorer-only. The candidate infers rows at 0.25×text-height
tolerance and cell gaps above 2×adjacent text height; associates a patient's DOB column with the
next table row or an empty DOB label with the immediately adjacent date-only line. Third-party
labels and body-section boundaries end patient context. Conflicting, missing or ambiguous DOBs
remain unknown; names never override a DOB contradiction. Original criteria remain zero false
stops on correct/unknown files and at least 90% detection of wrong-DOB files. Freeze source and
manifests before held-out scoring; independent verification is required.

  recorded command in a fresh external directory; all 28 request rows and the fixture receipt
  exactly match the runner's result.

  precisely N reads, delivered pages, retained records and proposed page locators with no
  duplicates or omissions; exactly ceil(N/U) extracted plan units, batches and proposals; zero
  remaining units, pending windows, tool errors or accepted documents; and final reason

  N23/U15 has 15+8 retained records, N23/U20 has 20+3, N800/U15 has 53×15+5, and the U30
  final units have 20 records. The tail calculation is therefore exercised by actual host

  counts, inclusive field counters and structural partitions. Every per-request partition
  sums exactly to the measured serialized text; its split user/tool groups reconcile with

  that all older results are stale. All 14 matrix cells have 5,344 maximum media-string bytes;
  the two N23 tail checks have 5,336. The request model and U+3 first-proposal model agree in
  all 16 cells. All four repeated N200/N800 U2/U10 request-measurement row sequences agree
  with earlier completed receipts after excluding newly added partition/model annotations.
- Resume: independently checked that only N800/U2 needed one extension. Its initial 1,024
  requests / 830,087,567 characters / 671 reads / 670 records / 335 accounted units remain
  separately recorded. Before/after snapshots retain the same nonempty checkpoint, identity,

  198 requests / 157,537,314 characters after Resume. Earlier original U2/U10 raw receipt

  of **15.261% / 18.120%** from U10's 393,189,513; both exceed the frozen 10% exploratory

  from request 33 to 53. Independently checked the plot's data/area formulas and rendered

- Scope: **F1's declared execution and verification are complete**, including the tail checks,

  All five code hashes, twelve artifact hashes and six source-PDF hashes match. The first saved

  U15/U20's residual model remains explicitly unvalidated. Code checks reported usage after a

  a noninferiority pass. Critical extra narrative still requires the declared independent
  manual audit; the literal grader does not establish its absence.
- Scope: this verifies prerequisite measurements and the frozen execution/scoring protocol,

## F7 results — preserved primary cohort and corpus correction

### Run 2026-09-25 — coordinator, primary cohort and historical regressions

- Environment: the common baseline, Node v24.19.0, macOS arm64; production PDF worker's
  `identity` action for the unchanged T21 baseline, actual PDF.js text boxes for the candidate.
  The separate F7 prototype and all fictional PDFs/receipts remain outside Git. No provider
  requests, owner-profile access or accepted writes occurred.
- Commands, from the prototype: `node f7-generate.ts development`,
  `node f7-generate.ts heldout`, `node f7-run.ts development`, `node f7-run.ts freeze`,
  `node f7-run.ts heldout`, `node f7-run.ts regressions`, and `node f7-zip.ts`.
  Manifests record source hashes, intended labels and coordinates; those truth fields never
  enter classification. The freeze pins runtime, generator, scorer, baseline and both manifests.
  The date parser and known fictional Self DOB are unchanged from T21. Full source descriptions
  and the candidate's geometry/association rules are in the preregistered plan above.
- **Measured raw scores, separately identical in development and held-out:** the baseline
  detects 30/100 wrong-DOB entries; the candidate detects 90/100. Each makes zero false stops
  among 100 correct and 40 unknown entries. Candidate outcomes are unknown for 80/240 entries.
  The candidate recovers all 30 split-label, 30 separate-row table, 20 inline and 10 repeated
  header wrong-DOB entries. Ten deliberately ambiguous patient/subscriber mixtures remain
  unknown. That abstention must not be described as a clearly associated DOB contradiction.
- **Material corpus defect:** repeated case variations produced identical PDF bytes.
  Development has 158 distinct hashes, held-out 168, rather than 240 distinct sources each.
  After deduplicating by source hash, held-out wrong-DOB detection is baseline 20/60 versus
  candidate **50/60 = 83.33%**; development candidate is **40/50 = 80%**. Both remain below
  the 90% criterion on distinct sources. Each split still has zero stops on its 100 distinct
  correct and eight distinct unknown PDFs. The raw 90/100 score is retained as a weighted
  fixture result; it does **not** qualify the detector. No confidence interval treats copies
  as independent observations.
- Historical regressions: actual regenerated originals match the previous T21 PDF hashes.
  Baseline again detects 8/10 wrong-DOB files; candidate detects 10/10, repairing the table-row
  and split-value misses. Both have zero false stops among nine correct and three unknown files.
  The actual stored ZIP's three members independently produce match, unknown and clear
  contradiction. These known regressions support the targeted fix, not fresh generalization.
- Validation: three focused association tests and strict TypeScript checks pass. The first
  standalone typecheck reported an unused import and an implicit scorer-array type; these
  non-runtime issues were fixed before freeze. A generator-only prototype with the alternate
  label `Birth date` was preserved before the scored corpus used `Date of birth`; no held-out
  scores were opened during that setup correction. The candidate was not tuned after the
  primary holdout. Independent verification follows; the corpus weakness prompted the separate
  extension below rather than replacing these measurements.

### Verification 2026-09-25 — independent verifier, F7 primary cohort and regressions

- Method: inspected the preregistration, generator, runtime, unchanged T21 baseline and scorer;
  verified all frozen source/manifest hashes and all 480 primary PDF hashes; independently
  reclassified saved cells/text and recomputed raw, per-template and source-hash-deduplicated
  scores. Reduced actual-PDF reruns cover thirty held-out cases (each truth/template), all
  twenty-two original regressions and all three ZIP members: 55 files, with identical extraction
  and classification results. Original-regression PDF hashes also match the prior T21 files.
  No provider calls or private inputs were used.
- Agreement: **agree with the raw measurements, flag the corpus duplication as material**.
  Each 240-file split measures baseline 30/100 versus candidate 90/100 wrong-DOB detections,
  zero false stops among 100 correct and 40 unknown entries, and candidate unknown outcomes
  80/240. However, development contains only 158 distinct PDF hashes and held-out only 168.
  The held-out unique-source result is baseline 20/60 versus candidate **50/60 (83.33%)**
  wrong-DOB detections, with zero stops among 100 unique correct and eight unique unknown files.
  Development's unique-source candidate result is 40/50 (80%), also below 90%. Duplicate
  bytes cannot be counted as independent cases to qualify the detector. The original raw
  90/100 result remains valid only with its duplication/weighting qualification.
- Regression and archive checks: original T21 wrong-DOB detection improves from 8/10 to
  10/10, with zero false stops among nine correct and three unknown files. These are known
  regression fixtures, not fresh held-out evidence. The actual three-member archive still
  produces per-member match / unknown / clear contradiction; no archive-wide identity decision
  is inferred from one member.
- Method limits: candidate classification receives extracted coordinates/text and Self DOB,
  not oracle truth, template number, document seed or expected row boundaries. Conflicting or
  ambiguous patient dates and recognized third-party roles fail open. The frozen candidate
  improves the two targeted layout misses, but these results do not establish production
  safety or detection on arbitrary document layouts. A separately frozen unique-source
  cohort is needed before any fresh-cohort qualification; it must not overwrite these results.

### Run 2026-09-25 — coordinator, separately reserved unique-source F7 extension

- The primary corpus and its deduplicated failure above remain intact. Before generating or
  scoring the extension, a new plan declared 100 wrong-DOB, 100 correct and 40 unknown sources
  per split, with a hard assertion that all 240 PDF hashes differ. The classifier is unchanged:
  `f7-runtime.ts` SHA-256 `1bbd09f1d4797650e847cedf5743856c355da1e15907c8c841f48f0c7b3e3538`.
  No algorithm or threshold was tuned against this new cohort.
- Corpus changes: printed names combine ten ordinary fictional given names and ten surnames;
  wrong DOBs now vary by case, independently of rendering. Development years use
  `2001 + i mod 20`, held-out `1947 + i mod 31`, month `1 + floor(i/28)`, day `13 + i mod 14`.
  Formats cycle ISO, unambiguous numeric and full English month names. Correct dates remain
  the fictional Self DOB; differing names therefore exercise the rule that DOB agreement must
  not reject a changed name. Development uses Helvetica with a left origin of `44 + 2n`;
  held-out uses Times-Roman/Courier, `86 + 2n`, changed vertical spacing, and actual DOB-first
  table columns instead of patient-first columns. PDF object order also varies. Runtime receives
  the resulting ordinary PDF boxes, never the generator's column indices or intended identity.
- Structural case weights stay declared: 30 split label/value, 30 separate-row demographic
  table, 20 inline, 10 running-header and 10 ambiguous patient/subscriber wrong-source cases;
  the correct and unknown controls retain the roles described above. There are **240 distinct
  source hashes in each split**, with no primary source reused. Distinct sources do not imply
  240 independent structural templates; broad template generalization remains unknown.
- Commands: `node f7-generate-v2.ts development`, `node f7-generate-v2.ts heldout`,
  `node f7-run-v2.ts development`, `node f7-run-v2.ts freeze`,
  `node f7-run-v2.ts heldout`. Freeze timestamp `2026-09-25T04:38:56.227Z` precedes held-out
  scoring and pins all runtime/generator/scorer/baseline files and manifests. Generator hash
  `6f17baf05e5d73540f064d816492e0a2d2427ded39536b917074bd2322e64596`;
  scorer hash `fef25b06ea95ef9c53f0445300cb0e96142a8bc5e29f778a62eb4a8364c129d2`.
  Strict standalone TypeScript checks passed before the freeze. Raw artifacts remain external.
- **Measured development and held-out, separately:** baseline detects 30/100 wrong-DOB files;
  the unchanged candidate detects **90/100**, with **zero false stops on 100 correct and
  40 unknown files**. Candidate outcomes are unknown for 80/240 files. Every split-label,
  separate-row table, inline and repeated-header wrong-DOB case is detected. All ten
  intentionally ambiguous patient/subscriber cases abstain; their printed relationship remains
  ambiguous even though authoring knows the intended wrong patient.
- Against criteria: the reserved unique-source corpus meets the bounded 90% detection and
  zero-false-stop thresholds. The same targeted classifier also repaired both historical misses.
  This establishes a useful follow-up candidate for the tested layouts, not universal identity
  safety, an OCR-based decision or permission to enable a production stop. The primary duplicate
  corpus remains a failed qualification attempt. Independent verification is still required.

### Verification 2026-09-25 — independent verifier, F7 unique-source extension

- Method: independently checked the new preregistration, generator/scorer and freeze against
  the unchanged primary runtime hash. Verified every new PDF hash and both manifest hashes,
  re-derived all 480 baseline/candidate outcomes and per-template counts from saved evidence,
  and reran extraction plus both classifiers on thirty held-out PDFs spanning every truth and
  template category. Held-out scoring follows the recorded freeze; no provider calls were made.
- Agreement: **agree**. Each split has exactly 240 unique PDF hashes, all 480 differ across
  development/held-out, and none reuses a primary-cohort source hash. Both splits independently
  measure baseline 30/100 versus candidate **90/100 wrong-DOB detections**, zero stops among
  100 correct and 40 unknown sources, and 80/240 candidate unknown outcomes. All thirty reduced
  actual-PDF reruns match the saved text, cells and classifications exactly.
- The detected wrong-DOB cases comprise 30 split label/value, 30 separate-row table, 20 inline
  and 10 repeated-header cases. All ten mixed patient/subscriber cases abstain. Inspection
  confirms held-out table columns really place the DOB before the patient name, and runtime
  classification infers their positions from the PDF rather than receiving generator indices.
- Decision: **pass the reserved corpus's fixed 90% detection and zero-false-stop thresholds**.
  This completes the optional F7 follow-up as a bounded prototype experiment, alongside its
  separately verified historical-regression and per-ZIP-member checks. Distinct PDF bytes do
  not make the small set of structural templates independent population evidence. The primary
  deduplicated failure remains intact; this extension does not establish universal identity
  safety, OCR performance or permission to activate a production stop. Overall follow-up
  completeness remains pending the other declared experiments.

Completion includes retained failures and bounded conclusions; it does not mean every candidate passed.
