- **F3 partly measured:** development and historical regressions are independently checked below. The 1,440-call held-out cold/warm-host matrix and reduced independent OCR reruns remain pending. Deskew's development qualification coexists with five historical pages above the 3% CER limit and incomplete critical-content retention.
- **F6 prepared, not executed:** the independently reviewed diagnostic pipeline design below retains failed component/adoption gates. Development, held-out scoring and independent result verification remain pending.

The owner requested a positive independent score for completeness and extendedness. That review will evaluate the original request against the full written execution record after the outstanding work; no overall positive execution grade is claimed here.

## F2 results — primary matrix launch and continuation protocol

### F2 primary matrix — frozen protocol and launch, 2026-09-25

Status: **launched; results pending**. This entry records the preregistered live matrix and preserved development attempts. It does not claim completed F2 quality, latency, cost, or noninferiority results. The F1 prerequisite completed all 14 primary cells and both tail checks before this launch.

The primary plan was frozen at 04:23:05 UTC with SHA-256 `76c71649c8e672add47b3d199afd65f388fd60360dfdf9402f1e3ee22c7c9dd5`. All 120 cells remain scheduled: six independently fictional heldout source PDFs × U2/U10/U15/U20 × five repeats. Two dense sources have 40 pages and 160 authored observation records each; two mixed sources have 40 pages and 24 records each; two single-report sources have 200 pages and one report containing six distributed facts each. Development and heldout sources use distinct text/layout details. Unit order rotates across documents and repeats according to the saved balanced schedule; it is not selected from observed quality. The first scheduled cell is `heldout-dense-0-r1-u2`.

This tests the current application's fixed-unit plans: the harness precreates the ordinary intake plan, creates the conversion chat, links it before sending the production start message, and attaches the normal `IntakeBatchManager`. `ProxyModelBridge`, PDF upload/read, tool calls and proposal publication execute against the qualified live route. This is not a host-pushed replacement processing architecture. Fictional identity name and date of birth are seeded equally across arms; record answers and grading oracles never enter model prompts or runtime tools. There is no accepted clinical write.

The qualified alias resolves to `gpt-6-astra`; application prompt-cache markers are disabled and reasoning is unset, while any provider-reported automatic cached-input usage is still measured. The fixed calibration adds 1,624 adapter-prefix input tokens per request. The matched development media probes measured 178 and 74 additional input tokens for the two one-page native PDFs; the declared selection uses the maximum, 178 tokens/page, with the cost model's default two media exposures/page. Media extrapolation beyond those native-PDF probes remains unvalidated. U15/U20 use the existing U10 residual estimate, also explicitly unvalidated; the structural model is not a timing or quality prediction.

The 120 modeled input-plus-output totals sum to 1,709,410,550 tokens, with per-cell estimates from 3,467,966 to 59,470,547. These are structural estimates, **not measured usage or money spent**. Each cell retains its own declared stop boundary of twice its modeled total. For the first dense U2 cell, the estimate is 12,469,942 and its boundary is 24,939,884 reported tokens. After a response crosses that boundary, no next model request may be sent. Missing successful-response usage also stops the cell. Ordinary application request, token, slice and read guards remain active. Explicit Resume is allowed only after progress, with remaining work and within the same cell boundary; it does not supply a new allowance. Failed and incomplete cells stay in the results and cannot support a cheap-winner claim.

The harness retains every actual request/response, reported input/output/cached usage, request duration, sanitized rate-limit/Retry-After headers, tool events, actual PDF page-read receipts, publication events, grade snapshots and final records. Completion requires exhausted reading, zero remaining units and pending read windows, actual read receipts for every page, unchanged active plan ID/membership, unchanged original SHA-256 and zero accepted document/observation/medication/procedure rows. First valid review and 50%-of-expected valid review are observed after publication using 100 ms polling plus scheduling and grader latency; they are discovery timestamps, not exact UI rendering times.

Five development smoke attempts remain separately preserved, in their original order:

1. `development-smoke-1790309572359`: no request, no review record, incomplete. A precreated plan was treated as already reviewable by ordinary queue startup. Its original receipt remains unchanged.
2. `development-smoke-1790309633556`: one aborted request, no record, incomplete. Startup occurred before the conversion link/checkpoint was established. Reported usage is zero because no usage receipt returned; actual usage is unknown, not proven zero.
3. `development-smoke-1790309788807`: one aborted request, no record, incomplete. Linking after startup was still too late for the ordinary intake checkpoint guard. Again no usage receipt returned, so measured zero is only a lower bound.
4. `development-smoke-1790309833218`: one-page workflow completed in 51,676.605 ms with four requests, 60,553 input + 1,365 output = 61,918 reported tokens, including 17,536 cached input. One record was returned but the then-current literal grade was 0/1. Ambiguous authored encounter-date wording and the exact duplicated-unit representation were investigated; this receipt and its original score were not rewritten.
5. `development-smoke-1790310048882`: after the development-only fixture and grading clarifications, the one-page workflow completed in 60,078.977 ms with five requests, 73,697 input + 1,598 output = 75,295 reported tokens, including 9,600 cached input. Grade was 1/1 correct, precision 1, zero duplicates, all original/plan/read/zero-accepted-write checks passed. First valid review was observed at 52,805.703 ms. This qualifies the live harness smoke, not the heldout matrix.

The final grading rule was fixed before primary execution: a numeric `valueText` may be either the exact authored numeric literal or that literal followed by the exact unit when the separate unit field also matches. Reformatting numbers, wrong units and extra qualifiers do not receive credit. Duplicate count means excess copies, with duplicate groups separately reported. Person/date association, exact factual recall, literal precision and wrong-person proposals are recorded. Whole-report original/page-1 linkage is the document-provenance endpoint; individual finding-page localization is reported separately and is not implied by the former. Critical narrative extras still require independent manual audit. Exact whole-document bootstrap enumeration keeps each document's five repeats together; there are only six document clusters overall, two per family and four for cross-page association. Pointwise intervals are not simultaneous guarantees. Missing runs, undefined precision and incomplete pairs cannot satisfy the declared bounded noninferiority gate.

Before launch, repository formatting, full TypeScript checks and seven focused fixture/summary tests passed. A separate verifier reviewed the frozen primary plan and prerequisite receipts; see the independent F2 prerequisite/frozen-protocol verification entry. Runner-authored primary outcomes still require separate result verification.

Portable commands, from the isolated F2 worktree, were:

```sh
node src/scripts/experiment-f2-run.ts smoke
node src/scripts/experiment-f2-run.ts plan
node src/scripts/experiment-f2-run.ts run
```

The artifact root retains `run-plan.json`, `fixtures/manifest.json`, all six oracle manifests and PDFs, `grader-protocol.json`, media-calibration receipts, route-qualification receipts, every smoke directory and `runs/<cell-id>/`. The plan hashes the original PDFs, oracle/manifests, grader protocol, sanitized route/calibration receipts and five relevant source files. Frozen source hashes are:

- `experiment-f2-fixtures.ts`: `8b49ad6f7397ad213068ca151789e38f5cec0703d41b4d54b0117ec0907d0528`.

#### Predeclared external-interruption continuation

The original primary process remains running. A separate continuation/controller implementation was independently reviewed before any continuation was attempted. Two review rounds corrected wall-time anchoring, exact frozen-cap/receipt reconciliation, persisted-state checks, normal current-index reset after partial publication, component usage reconciliation and unknown-budget qualification. The verifier independently reran seven offline tests, including a real assistant/batch-manager test with one retained proposal, simulated proxy failure, manager recreation, exact same chat/checkpoint/original/proposals, and zero accepted writes. The pre-use review passed; actual live continuation is still unproven.

Declarations were written at 04:46:54–55 UTC. `continuation-protocol.json` binds continuation source SHA-256 `f5c0bacc2211219ecd932a0722df861defcf37e6066aa2862ee8f8f990db1ca2`, collector/controller source SHA-256 `e49f3ee14d3ef0137f9be5a20c91b4a89281a42f204e79dad7d8c7a0e206b375`, and the unchanged primary plan. `control-protocol.json` also binds continuation-protocol SHA-256 `2ceba7e129568db40c2db56909f1b2ff6d1543bd11b65e9cd9d7eeaee9491120`. No frozen primary source was edited.

After an external interruption, the approved controller may run only once the original primary process has exited. It visits the exact original 120-cell schedule, resumes the earliest eligible incomplete cell before a new cell, and reuses the same retained database/chat/batch/plan with cumulative requests, usage, page reads and the original 2× boundary. Unknown usage from failed or aborted requests is an unresolved budget requiring separately reviewed reconciliation, never a free retry. A token-overrun stop is not reset. Original and earlier continuation receipts remain immutable under numbered continuation directories. The collector selects the latest valid cumulative terminal receipt once per cell, retains every attempt by path/hash, and reports total wall duration, active duration and external wait separately. Missing/gapped chains or unfinished receipts block dispatch. It masks completion for summary qualification when budget usage is unresolved.

Approved operational commands, with declarations already completed, are:

```sh
node src/scripts/experiment-f2-control.ts collect
# Only after confirming the original primary process has exited:
node src/scripts/experiment-f2-control.ts run --primary-stopped
```

The external coordinator handoff contains exact artifact locations, process ownership, declaration receipts and commands. This launch/protocol entry remains pending until all primary cells have terminal recorded outcomes and a separate verifier reconstructs the results. No choice among U2/U10/U15/U20 is justified by launch or smoke success alone.

## Independent verification — F2 continuation before use

### Verification 2026-09-25 — independent verifier, F2 continuation and cumulative collection before use

**Agree, limited to the revised offline protocol and pre-use checks.** I inspected the continuation and controller sources, rechecked all 5 original frozen code hashes and 12 artifact hashes, and independently ran `node --test src/scripts/experiment-f2-control.test.ts src/scripts/experiment-f2-continue.test.ts`: **7 tests passed, 0 failed**. These tests use fictional local fixtures and an offline synthetic model bridge; I made no provider requests.

The real intake-manager test publishes a proposal, injects a route failure, recreates the manager, observes the valid current-index reset from 1 to 0, and checks the same original, intake, chat/checkpoint and proposal remain, with zero accepted records. The collector rejects changed caps, replaced prior request prefixes, missing continuation receipts, gapped numbering, doubled cumulative totals and altered input/output/cache subtotals. A failed request without usage remains an unresolved budget and forces the qualified completion flag false even when application processing finished. The collector includes exactly one latest cumulative result per planned cell, retaining earlier receipt paths and hashes rather than adding cumulative attempts twice.

Earlier review found and the runner corrected: lost setup time in the continuation wall-clock anchor; a latest-receipt cell/cap validation gap; an overly strict batch comparison that rejected the legitimate index reset; incomplete usage-component reconciliation; and a completion flag that could conceal unknown failed-request usage. The final sources also compare retained state before dispatch and constrain Resume changes, preserve the original cumulative cap and request history, separate active time from external wait, and bind the new protocols to the unchanged original plan.

**Scope and remaining conditions:** this clears the unchanged protocols for declaration and later controlled use; it does not establish that a live provider continuation has succeeded. The manager test recreates the manager, not the entire live orchestration path. The original matrix process must be confirmed stopped before starting the replacement controller. Unknown usage on any failed or aborted request still blocks continuation pending separately reviewed reconciliation; no zero-cost assumption or fresh budget is authorized. The 120-cell matrix and overall follow-up completeness remain ungraded while execution is unfinished.

Reviewed continuation SHA-256: `f5c0bacc2211219ecd932a0722df861defcf37e6066aa2862ee8f8f990db1ca2`; controller SHA-256: `e49f3ee14d3ef0137f9be5a20c91b4a89281a42f204e79dad7d8c7a0e206b375`.

## F3 results — completed development and regression measurements

### F3 runner interim result — development and original regressions complete; held-out execution in progress

**Status:** incomplete. This entry records completed measurements and the frozen selection, not a finished F3 experiment. The full 60-page held-out matrix remains running; no held-out quality result or broad adoption claim is made. Independent verification of the completed development/regression results is pending.

- The final V2 development matrix completed 60 pages ×4 arms =240 calls. At 300 dpi with pinned CLI Tesseract 5.3.0, Debian English traineddata 1:4.1.0-2, PSM3 and one Docker CPU, baseline CER was 27,948/180,450 =15.4879%; deskew 1,834/180,450 =1.01635%; order reconstruction 27,540/180,450 =15.2618%; both 1,162/180,450 =0.643946%. Pages above 3% CER were respectively 15/60, 0/60, 16/60 and 0/60.
- Total CPU median/maximum, including host preprocessing/reconstruction and OCR: baseline 2.48/3.63 s; deskew 3.30/4.83 s; order 5.38/9.41 s; both 6.27/9.81 s. The actual pixel-derived ruling detector makes reconstruction costly on these dense pages. The original five-second target is retained; the lower CER of the combined arm does not erase its CPU failure.
- Development paragraph omissions were 60/360 for baseline/order and 0/360 for deskew/both. Exact critical-content occurrences retained were 1,046/1,340 for baseline/order and 1,328/1,340 for deskew/both. No arm introduced a critical loss versus its same-page baseline. The 12 remaining absolute losses in deskew/both are still failures to recover authored content. Paired OCR word hashes were identical, permitting attribution of ordering-only differences to reconstruction.
- The fixed development rule selected **deskew alone**, the sole eligible arm. The selection and source/data/image freeze were written at 2026-09-25 04:30:13 UTC, before held-out execution. Runtime hash: `e8bab3a503ba62109315dd25ea278156f7697ca5b01da7aa92617b88877dc6ca`; image ID: `sha256:8d784a58d02c25943b7c7b785d557b94827c493d22b6d29ee1f2dc0d511ed568`. The 392,656,197-byte image adds 47,193,890 bytes over the retained T18 CLI image. No held-out result changes this selection.
- The 15 original 300 dpi T18 regression pages ×4 arms also completed. Baseline CER was 1,129/11,270 =10.0177%; deskew 230/11,270 =2.04082%; order 929/11,270 =8.24312%; both 30/11,270 =0.266193%. Pages above 3% were 7/15, 5/15, 2/15 and 0/15. CPU maxima were 1.50, 1.62, 4.25 and 4.98 s. Thus the combined arm repairs the older simple pages within the CPU limit while failing the limit on denser development documents; deskew still misses five original regression pages' CER bar.
- The frozen primary regression oracle lacked critical labels, so its critical-retention endpoint remains unmeasured. A separate **post-hoc diagnostic**, declared before scoring those fields, selected all generator-authored labeled dates, Marker-A/B/C value-unit rows and the marginal note. Every arm retained 55/55 dates and 15/15 value-unit rows, but only 1/5 complete marginal notes: 71/75 total each, with zero newly introduced losses. The old fixtures contain no negation fact. This supplemental result does not rewrite the primary oracle, selection or preregistration and illustrates a shared loss that a relative-to-baseline criterion can miss.
- Before held-out execution, independent preflight found and repaired a missing runtime table guard, omitted failure strata/unknown denominators, repeat-timing summaries and memory-scope/cache issues. V1's 240 development calls remain retained separately. V2 uses detected image rulings to guard row ordering; unruled or missed-ruling tables are not universally qualified. The final held-out protocol is 60 pages ×4 arms ×3 cold repeats plus three persistent-host repeats =1,440 calls, all sequential at one CPU. A persistent Node host still launches a fresh Tesseract process per page; it does not mean a warmed OCR engine.
- Reproduction uses `f3-generate.ts`, `f3-runtime.ts`, `f3-run.ts`, `f3-score.ts`, `f3-freeze.ts` and `Dockerfile.f3` in the isolated prototype worktree. Generate to an external artifact directory with `node f3-generate.ts "$ARTIFACTS"`, build the CLI-derived image, run/score `development`, freeze it, then run/score `regression` and run `holdout cold` followed by `holdout warm`. The generator reuses the original T18 fictional regression fixtures at its declared external location. The freeze contains seven source/build hashes, all 135 images/oracles, traineddata and image identity; font and canvas-version receipts distinguish exact-image reruns from re-rendering on another host. Raw outputs remain outside Git. Supplemental commands are `node f3-regression-critical.ts "$ARTIFACTS" plan` and then `score`.
- `npm run format:check`, `npm run typecheck` and focused prototype TypeScript checks passed. No product behavior, Git history, prior result entry or experiment checkbox was changed. Full held-out scoring and independent result verification remain outstanding.

## Independent verification — F3 development and regressions

### Verification 2026-09-25 — independent verifier, F3 development and historical regressions

**Agree with the saved-output measurements and development selection; holdout qualification remains pending.** I read the F3 plan, generator, runtime, runner, scorer and freeze logic, then independently recomputed sequence-sensitive edit distances, literal critical-field occurrence counts, paragraph alignment/omission and logical-order statistics from the retained text. I reconciled every aggregate/stratum, timing numerator, page maximum and threshold count with its individual receipts. This covers 240 final development arm/page runs, 60 historical-regression runs and the 240 retained earlier development runs: 540 saved-output checks. I did not launch an OCR container or inspect heldout quality while its timed matrix was running.

- **Freeze and controls:** all seven frozen source hashes, the plan and manifest hashes, and all 135 input/oracle hash pairs match. The 15 historical input files exactly match the earlier T18 300-dpi images. The freeze timestamp is `2026-09-25T04:30:13.290Z`; the first heldout job file was created at `04:33:46.221Z`. Runtime receives the opaque input path, arm and output prefix only. Its angle search uses image pixels; reconstruction uses recognized word boxes and image-detected rulings, not expected text, labels or oracle boxes. The source fixes Tesseract 5.3.0, English data, 300 dpi, PSM 3, one CPU, 2 GiB and no network. Recorded recognized-word hashes agree for baseline/order and deskew/both on every checked page. I also parsed each raw TSV, matched its words to the receipt, and confirmed reconstruction retains the complete word multiset; non-ordering arms retain the original Tesseract text.
- **Final development, 60 pages per arm:** baseline has 27,948 edits / 180,450 characters = **15.48795% CER**; deskew 1,834 / 180,450 = **1.01635%**; order 27,540 / 180,450 = **15.26185%**; both 1,162 / 180,450 = **0.64395%**. Pages exceeding 3% are **15 / 0 / 16 / 0** respectively. Maximum total CPU is **3.63 / 4.83 / 9.41 / 9.81 seconds**; counts exceeding 5 seconds are **0 / 0 / 48 / 60**. These figures reproduce the frozen selection of **deskew as the only development-eligible arm**. Both has lower CER but misses the CPU criterion; selecting it as the qualifying winner would be incorrect.
- **Development content retention:** baseline/order retain 1,046 of 1,340 annotated literal occurrences; deskew/both retain 1,328. Paragraph omissions are **60 / 0 / 60 / 0** out of 360. No arm introduces a critical occurrence loss relative to its own page's baseline. That relative criterion does not mean perfect critical retention: deskew/both still miss twelve exact negation-clause occurrences across four perspective-distorted table pages. Exact logical blocks remain unmatched on **123 / 4 / 135 / 20** occasions out of 700; zero measured order inversions applies only to matched blocks and does not erase these unmatched blocks or the nonzero CER.
- **Historical regressions, 15 pages per arm:** baseline **1,129 / 11,270 = 10.01775% CER**, deskew **230 / 11,270 = 2.04082%**, order **929 / 11,270 = 8.24312%**, both **30 / 11,270 = 0.26619%**. Pages exceeding 3% are **7 / 5 / 2 / 0**. CPU maxima are **1.50 / 1.62 / 4.25 / 4.98 seconds**. Deskew therefore improves aggregate regression CER but still fails the per-page criterion on five historical pages; its maximum is 4.03633%. Both's regression success does not override its development CPU failure.
- **Separate post-hoc critical-label supplement:** the original regression oracles still have empty critical labels and their primary critical comparison remains unknown. The supplemental plan was created at `04:47:58.517Z`, before its scored result at `04:48:07.473Z`, but after OCR execution. I independently reproduced the declared token-occurrence scoring from saved outputs: every arm retains **55/55 labeled dates, 15/15 value-unit rows and 1/5 complete marginal notes = 71/75** occurrences, with zero newly introduced losses relative to baseline. The four missing marginal notes remain failures. The old sources contain no annotated negation fact, so this supplement does not measure historical negation retention. It does not retroactively replace the frozen primary labels or make the regression corpus a fresh holdout.

The retained earlier development outputs also reproduce; they are development history, not additional independent holdout observations. Their ordering arms had the same quality totals but different implementation/timing, so their lower CPU figures must not qualify the final runtime. The final corpus's 60 pages represent twelve underlying documents with five distortions each, not sixty independent templates. Font-family/file hashes are recorded; re-rendering on a different font environment produces a new fixture. Handwritten-font cases remain simulated. The paragraph metric uses its declared 0.5 token-alignment threshold, and literal critical retention is not a complete semantic correctness test.

Timing here is one cold run per development/regression arm/page. CPU includes Node preprocessing/reconstruction and child OCR within the container; process RSS is a maximum individual-process high-water mark, not simultaneous summed memory. Cold process timing does not include Docker launch latency. The planned warm mode retains the Node worker but starts a fresh Tesseract process per page. Three-repeat heldout timing, warm/cold comparison, heldout quality/strata, and reduced actual OCR reruns remain outstanding before a complete F3 verification. This entry gives no positive whole-program execution score.

Independent verification artifacts are `verify.mts`, `verification.json` and `additional-checks.json` under the external F3 verifier root. Run the TypeScript verifier with Node 24 against the recorded prototype source and artifact roots; it reads raw saved outputs and writes only its own verification receipt. Its edit-distance implementation and aggregate calculations are separate from the runner's scorer. No original evidence was overwritten.

## F6 results — diagnostic preparation

### F6 diagnostic launch design — component versions fixed; execution not yet started

**Status:** not executed. The owner-authorized diagnostic extension preserves the original F6 adoption prerequisite and its failed/deferred outcome when F3–F5 fail. This preparation is not evidence that the combined pipeline works. F3's full outcomes will be recorded before the F6 matrix starts.

- The frozen plan retains 20 development and 20 new held-out fictional document pairs, five separately authored template families per split, four pairs per family, four pages per export, and a baseline/re-export pair per document. Every re-export contains seven unchanged clinical passages, one critical correction and one new passage. New values, fonts, names, templates and seeds separate the splits. Value/unit/date/negation/deleted-fact corrections are balanced across families but remain confounded with family, so this does not isolate edit-type difficulty.
- Four image arms cross baseline versus development-selected deskew OCR with baseline versus experimental automatic matching. Two actual native-PDF text-layer controls isolate matching/layout problems from OCR. The selected OCR is fixed from F3 development, even if F3 later fails a held-out bar. Experimental matching uses the frozen F4v2 line reconstruction, literal token differences and F5 index-supported cross-page continuation. The unresolved within-passage column family and unassigned/new-content fragmentation remain in the corpus.
- The full scope is 640 actual OCR calls: 40 pairs ×2 exports ×4 pages ×2 OCR variants, shared only across matcher arms. Calls will use the pinned F3 image, one Docker CPU and no runtime network. Runtime receives source files and an earlier-import index, never generator angles, truth spans, partner IDs or expected edit locations. Oracle labels are not mounted in OCR containers. Native geometry comes from actual PDF.js extraction. Equal image OCR can yield only related/uncertain, never an unchanged-original decision; every re-export is read in full.
- Scoring requires one-to-one geometric/literal region assignment, correct earlier document **and** clinical passage identity, retention of new automatically segmented passages, strict planted-edit localization and independent authored date-role labels. Missing OCR counts as a downstream miss. Strict edit recovery is distinct from literal value/unit/date/negation retention; the latter is scoped to the changed passage with token boundaries, so another encounter cannot supply a missing value. Deletion absence is unknown when the changed passage is insufficiently recovered. The known-span k10/w6/0.8 containment metric is retained only as a secondary diagnostic, never a replacement for automatic precision or usable-index coverage.
- A 5×4 development grid uses minimum page OCR confidence in `{0,70,80,90,95}` and maximum unassigned dark-pixel share in `{1,0.6,0.4,0.2}`. Select one point using only development experimental-OCR/experimental-matcher outcomes, maximizing usable accepted-document coverage subject to zero accepted hidden critical corrections; apply it unchanged to all four image arms. Report every held-out grid point, accepted/usable/abstained denominators, false-confident documents and conditional risk. Zero acceptance cannot pass the 80% useful-coverage criterion. The original native precision≥0.99/recall≥0.95, zero false-unchanged planted corrections, span recall≥0.9 and zero false-exclusion bars remain unchanged.
- Independent pre-execution audit found and resolved insufficient template distinctness, self-derived date truth, omitted partial changed-fragment false-unchanged counts, cross-encounter critical-field contamination and substring value matching. The reviewed runtime/scorer has no remaining reported blocker. All governing dates are newer than the fixed cutoff, so old-only date selectivity remains outside this corpus. New-content coverage means a complete indexed region survives; an automatic novelty classifier or user retrieval interface is not implemented.
- Sources are `f6-generate.ts`, `f6-run.ts`, `f6-index.ts`, `f6-features.ts`, `f6-score.ts`, `f6-freeze.ts`, and copied frozen fingerprint/date/PDF writer modules. Plans, copied-module hashes, F3 selection/image identity and font environment are recorded externally. Generate both splits before scoring, run development, then freeze source/input/oracle hashes and the development-selected risk point before held-out execution. The full F6 freeze and selected risk point are **pending development execution**, not claimed complete here. Root format/typecheck and focused prototype checks passed. Independent verification of results remains required after execution.

## Independent verification — F6 before execution

### Independent F6 pre-execution review

Reviewer: separate fingerprint/live-runner agent; F6 generator, runtime and scorer were authored by the OCR/pipeline agent. This is a code and experimental-design review before F6 execution, not a verification of unobserved results.

Reviewed `f6-generate.ts`, `f6-index.ts`, `f6-features.ts`, `f6-score.ts`, `f6-run.ts`, and `f6-freeze.ts` in the isolated F6 worktree, along with the declared F6 plan. Runtime input uses PDFs/images and a runtime manifest; candidate identifiers come from source filenames and automatically inferred regions. Truth geometry, planted edits and expected date labels are used only by the scorer. OCR containers receive source images and jobs, not oracle files. The F3 component image and selected development-only OCR parameters are pinned. Native controls and all four image OCR×matcher combinations remain present.

The following concrete issues were identified and fixed by the author before F6 generation or scoring:

- Nominal template-family labels initially repeated most narrative content. The author supplied five distinct development and five distinct heldout clinical titles, introductions and narratives.
- Expected date roles initially reused the tested parser, risking shared blind spots. Expected roles now come directly from authored date labels and literal values; parser output on clean source text is a separate control.
- False-unchanged counts initially depended on full-span assignment. The scorer now counts unchanged decisions overlapping the known changed source geometry even when segmentation fragments that passage.
- Literal critical-field diagnostics initially could credit matching text elsewhere in the document. Evidence is now restricted to the actual old/new changed source span, independently of successful segmentation; deletion absence becomes unknown below 90% changed-span token coverage.
- Raw substring comparison could credit `5.4` inside `15.4`. Contiguous literal-token subsequence matching now preserves numeric boundaries.

Final reviewed behavior: one-to-one geometry plus literal-LCS matching counts missing true spans and extra predicted regions. Correct candidates require both the prior document and the correct clinical span. Strict critical-edit recovery requires the changed label, correct reference and emitted edit positions covering the planted correction; separate field-retention diagnostics distinguish this from simple OCR survival. Image-derived equal text is classified as related, never unchanged. Risk thresholds are selected from development outcomes only, then applied unchanged to heldout image arms; empty acceptance does not satisfy the 80% usable-coverage bar.

No outstanding blocking issue remained in the reviewed pre-execution implementation. This does not imply F6 passes its outcome bars. Results need a separate independent reconstruction or reduced rerun after execution. Preserve limitations: correction type remains confounded with template family, all authored governing dates are newer than the cutoff (so old-only exclusion selectivity is untested), and strict critical-edit recovery includes classification/localization requirements beyond literal source-text retention.

## Independent verification — F2 supplemental identity audit

### Supplemental audit plan and interim observation 2026-09-25 — independent verifier, F2 person linkage and review readiness

This supplemental audit was fixed at `2026-09-25T04:55:30.454Z` during execution, after inspecting an interim 152-record snapshot from the first dense U2 cell. That initial observation is explicitly post-hoc. The fixed audit rules apply to later retained snapshots/cells without changing the original runtime, fixtures, primary grader, quality margins or token cap. No provider requests or accepted writes are part of this audit.

The audit distinguishes three outcomes: **raw person linkage** through each record's unique context and exact source-supported name/DOB anchor; **host identity-established** status with matching evidenced name/DOB and no identity block; and **review-selectable** status. These are distinct from the frozen primary literal-record endpoint. For each supplemental outcome, the verifier will derive first and 50%-expected-record discovery times from the saved snapshots. An absent milestone is reported as not reached through the observed interval, never as zero time or as the primary latency. Terminal audits also check actual PDF facts/locators, raw context and payload narrative, saved proposal bytes, full coverage, zero accepted clinical records, and cumulative request-level input/output/cache usage. Unknown failed-request usage remains an unresolved budget.

**Interim observation, not a completed cell:** at 1,859,969.490 ms of the first dense U2 run, 152 of 160 expected records were present. Independent source comparisons found no value, unit, date, reference-interval or issuing-system errors in those records. All 152 projected literal record texts and all 190 raw model payloads (152 records plus 38 contexts) are supported by their corresponding actual source pages. Each of the 152 raw records links to a unique same-page context containing the correct printed fictional name and birth date. Manual inspection of the model clinical field shapes, report anchors and coverage notes found no additional unsupported diagnosis, medication, procedure or critical narrative in this retained portion. Apparent medication/procedure labels in some generic host projections were not supplied as such by the raw model proposals.

However, **all 152 host records have `subject: unknown`, identity `confirmation_required`, and `selectable: false`; none has host-established identity**. Raw contexts preserve the printed name/DOB, but the model supplied no structured `selfSuggestion` entry and left clinical subject unknown. First and 50% identity-established/selectable milestones had therefore not been reached through this snapshot. These remain legitimate proposals for user review and identity confirmation; being unselectable does not itself mean the transcription is false. It does mean literal precision alone cannot establish broad person-association correctness or immediate acceptance readiness.

The dense fixture's frozen association denominator is zero, so its primary literal score neither measures nor passes the host person-association endpoint. The separately audited raw context links support a narrower conclusion about retained source association. Neither those links nor the absence of a wrong-person flag may silently replace the original association metric. Later mixed/single-report outcomes retain their own frozen association scoring. Primary “first valid review” time remains discovery of a proposal passing the declared literal grader, with these identity/readiness qualifications stated beside it.

For the 59 completed model responses captured through this snapshot, the verifier reconciled each response's reported usage to its request receipt: **3,639,834 input + 54,018 output = 3,693,852 tokens**, with 267,904 reported cached input tokens and no missing usage among those responses. A read-only database check at audit time found zero documents, observations, medications and procedures accepted. The cell was still running; later calls, coverage and terminal totals are not inferred from this snapshot.

The external audit artifacts are `supplemental-audit-plan.json`, `audit-cell.mts`, the timestamped cell snapshot audit and `first-cell-initial-supplement.json`. This is a supplemental method and partial-result observation, not completion of F2 or a positive whole-program execution score.

## F2 extension — terminal finalization diagnostic design

### Proposed plan, 2026-09-25 — fingerprint runner

This is a design and code-path finding, not an executed extension. The frozen 120-cell primary schedule continues unchanged. No primary receipt, primary source, controller declaration or result predicate is edited.

The first dense U2 cell exposes a terminal bookkeeping boundary: all 160 literal observations and all 40 actual page reads are retained, all 20 workflow units are accounted, no read windows remain, yet the normal 15-minute slice deadline records `time_limit`. Its strict primary `complete` stays false. The application batch is `complete/items_paused`, which is distinct from experiment completeness.

Relevant current code paths in the F2 worktree:

- `src/server/assistant.ts:95`: `READING_SLICE_MS = 15 * 60 * 1000`.
- `src/server/assistant.ts:2790`: a soft deadline preserves an in-flight provider response and guarded tool effect; the timer marks the deadline reached.
- `src/server/assistant.ts:2866`: `beforeRequest` blocks the next provider call with `ReadingDeadlineError`.
- `src/server/assistant.ts:3017`: the exit handler maps that error to `time_limit`.
- `src/server/assistant.ts:3311`: normal turn completion reconciles workflow-accounted units, then checks the deadline before choosing `reading_exhausted` or continuation.
- `src/server/intake-reading-budget.ts:136`: automatic continuation needs progress and remaining units or pending read windows, so an otherwise complete source does not automatically advance.
- `src/shared/intake-batch.ts:105`: a review-ready item paused for a non-exhausted reason remains explicitly resumable.
- `src/server/intake-batches.ts:880`: ordinary Resume requeues the same item and linked chat; only `job_limit` extends the application's cumulative reading budget.
- `src/server/intake-batches.ts:541`: an idle retained chat receives the ordinary resume prompt; it is not replaced by a new import.

Read-only inspection of the first retained chat found `seen.length=40`, `pending.length=0`, `completedUnits=[]`, and no `accountedUnits`, while the durable workflow contains all 20 accounted units. Consequently, a new normal model turn that simply completes could reconcile those existing units as newly accounted checkpoint progress and return `reading_exhausted` without any reread or new proposal. This is a code-supported possibility, not a promise of live model behavior. If checkpoint accounting were already synchronized, a no-op completion could instead yield `no_progress`; the extension must retain either result without forcing a pass.

Proposed preregistration, to freeze and independently review before any extension calls:

1. Run only after every scheduled primary cell has a terminal outcome. Select all primary cells by the same outcome-independent eligibility rule: strict completion false; terminal reason `time_limit` or `context_limit`; every actual page read; zero remaining units/pending windows; unchanged original/plan; zero accepted clinical rows; no unknown request usage; no token overrun. Preserve the list before extension execution. Do not choose cells based on literal grade, identity or whether a favorable result seems likely.
2. Use one ordinary explicit `manager.resume` attempt per eligible cell in original primary order, against the exact retained database/chat/batch. No new source, new plan, new identity confirmation or new token allowance. All inherited requests and provider-reported input/output/cache totals remain cumulative; the original 2× cell boundary remains authoritative.
3. Preserve original terminal receipts and append a separately named finalization receipt. Before dispatch, verify source/plan/identity/checkpoint/proposals/counters and original cumulative usage. Allow only the ordinary same-target queue/index reset. No runtime rewrite should force the `reading_exhausted` label.
4. The narrow success outcome is ordinary `reading_exhausted` with zero remaining windows/units and unchanged source/plan/accepted rows. Separately require and report zero additional physical reads, zero additional proposals/candidates/records, no literal grade loss, and no identity/selectability changes to qualify a pure finalization result. Unexpected extra reads/proposals, failure, `no_progress`, guard stop or unknown usage remain explicit outcomes; there is no second attempt to improve the score.
5. Report incremental requests/tokens/active time and total inherited-plus-extension usage, plus external wait separately. Preserve primary strict completion and primary latency; do not rewrite earlier results or substitute the extension into primary noninferiority comparisons. Supplemental finalization outcomes may explain an operational limitation, not rescue a failed primary bar retroactively.
6. Before live execution, run a separate offline actual-assistant/manager test with an injected monotonic clock crossing the deadline during the final publication, retain an acknowledged full source, then Resume through an offline bridge that completes without additional tools. This tests the hypothesized checkpoint-reconciliation path and the negative already-synchronized case without provider calls. Independently review the implementation, eligibility manifest, counter reconciliation and frozen limits before authorizing the live extension.

Known limitation: the first source still has 160 unknown-subject, identity-confirmation-required, unselectable proposals. A successful finalization label would not establish identity or acceptance readiness; those remain separate endpoints. No action in this extension may confirm identity or accept records.

## F2 extension — offline finalization checks and independent clearance

### F2 terminal-finalization diagnostic — offline implementation, 2026-09-25

Status: offline mechanics tested; live diagnostic not executed. This is a separate extension prompted by the first primary dense U2 cell's preserved `time_limit` outcome after all source pages and expected literal observations were retained. The primary 120-cell schedule, source/runtime code, original results, strict completion predicate and primary comparisons remain unchanged.

Two actual-assistant/actual-batch-manager tests used an independently fictional one-unit source and an offline synthetic bridge, with no provider requests. Both read the whole source, then moved the injected monotonic clock across the 15-minute slice boundary during its final guarded publication. Neither test rewrote the checkpoint to manufacture the expected branch.

- In the actual request-admission failure path, `beforeRequest` threw the reading deadline error and `onExit` recorded `time_limit`. The existing proposal and source were retained, with zero remaining work. The checkpoint had no reconciled accounted unit. Recreating the manager and issuing one ordinary Resume followed by normal model completion without tools produced `reading_exhausted`. No additional read/tool call, proposal or accepted document was produced.
- In the negative control, normal `turn/completed` first reconciled the workflow-accounted unit, then recorded `time_limit` because the deadline had expired. Recreating the manager and issuing the same no-tool Resume produced `no_progress`, not `reading_exhausted`. This verifies the distinction between missing checkpoint bookkeeping and an already synchronized no-op; it is not a universal promise that Resume will finalize every complete-looking source.

Two additional tests exercise selection/eligibility: selection refuses fewer than 120 terminal planned outcomes or any unfinished ordinary route-continuation chain; it binds the latest cumulative terminal receipt, including a completed ordinary continuation, and preserves original cell order. It does not select by literal quality or identity outcome. Missing actual page coverage, changed source/plan, accepted records, remaining source work, unknown request usage and exceeded original token cap make a cell ineligible.

The separate implementation is `src/scripts/experiment-f2-finalize.ts`, with tests in `src/scripts/experiment-f2-finalize.test.ts`. It will freeze its own protocol and source/test hashes before selection, then freeze all eligible IDs and every underlying primary attempt/receipt hash before any diagnostic call. It permits one ordinary `manager.resume` per eligible cell, using exactly the retained profile database/chat/batch/checkpoint and original cumulative 2× boundary. It retains failures, `no_progress`, guard stops and extra reads/proposals as outcomes. A pure finalization result additionally requires no added reads/proposals, unchanged full proposal/candidate/version/record content, unchanged literal grade, identity and selectability, unchanged source/plan, and zero accepted writes. Incremental and cumulative usage and active versus external-wait time remain separate. These supplemental outcomes will not overwrite or replace primary results.

Executed offline validation command:

```sh
node --test src/scripts/experiment-f2-finalize.test.ts
npm run typecheck:tools
```

All four tests, full repository formatting and full TypeScript checks passed. The first local test invocation failed at fixture setup because the test had not installed the existing fictional model-alias helper; no modeled processing or provider request occurred. Using that established helper fixed the offline fixture setup. A separate verifier independently reran all four tests and completed a positive pre-use review. After that review and the final full checks, the separate `finalization-protocol.json` was declared with source SHA-256 `fa28628bb1e1a9ac507b99bb8b0002a039a3271cd75c6af494692732e33aa523` and test SHA-256 `7d41e42c12970cee40cbf756b4a9a8629fe01414006ac72232bcafbf1b90b56e`, bound to the unchanged original plan and frozen collector. No eligible-cell manifest or live finalization has been executed. Selection and live execution must wait until all 120 primary cells have terminal outcomes and the primary process has exited. The diagnostic pauses on external route failure or unknown usage and retains that attempt; a later launch may visit only untouched eligible cells.

### Verification 2026-09-25 — independent verifier, finalization diagnostic before use

**Agree with the separate protocol and revised implementation; offline pre-use review passes.** I compared the finalization source against the already reviewed continuation implementation, inspected the selection/state/accounting changes and independently ran `node --test src/scripts/experiment-f2-finalize.test.ts`: **four tests passed, zero failed**, without provider calls. All five original frozen source hashes and twelve artifact hashes remain unchanged. Calling the read-only selection function against current live artifacts correctly refuses selection because the second primary cell is not terminal. The diagnostic has not been selected or executed.

The actual assistant/manager tests distinguish the two relevant deadline paths. After the last guarded publication, the request-gate/exit path leaves durable workflow progress not yet reflected in the checkpoint; one ordinary no-tool Resume then produces **`reading_exhausted`**. The genuine completed-turn path reconciles accounting before recording `time_limit`; its otherwise equivalent no-tool Resume produces **`no_progress`**. Both preserve the original, proposals and seen-read count with no new tools or acceptance. No manual checkpoint rewrite creates the negative control. These are offline branch demonstrations, not predictions that a live model will refrain from extra work.

Selection requires terminal outcomes for all 120 frozen primary cells, rejects unfinished continuation chains, binds the latest cumulative primary receipt and every attempt hash, and preserves original cell order without inspecting grade or identity. Eligibility excludes missing coverage, changed source/plan, accepted rows, unknown request usage, prior completion, harness stops and cap overruns. Tests exercise these exclusions and selection of the latest retained continuation. Before dispatch, the runtime rechecks retained state and permits only the ordinary same-intake Resume reset; exclusive finalization directories prevent a second attempt, including after an unfinished first attempt.

The revised pure-finalization predicate checks unchanged full records, candidates/versions, proposals, literal grade, identity/selectability and zero added physical reads, not merely the absence of new record IDs. Every finalization preserves inherited request history and the original cumulative 2× cap, reports incremental and cumulative usage and separates active time from external wait. Missing usage is explicitly a lower bound and prevents pure qualification. At this review's request, the campaign now pauses after an unresolved route failure or unknown usage; a later launch skips that retained failed attempt and visits only untouched eligible cells. Negative outcomes and extra work remain evidence, without retries to improve the result.

The unchanged implementation may be declared after its repository checks pass. **Eligibility selection and live execution must wait until all primary cells are terminal and the primary controller has exited.** The primary receipts, scores, latencies and noninferiority inputs remain untouched; a later diagnostic success cannot retrospectively qualify a failed primary cell. The real-model finalization path and the whole follow-up program remain unverified until their results exist.

Reviewed SHA-256: finalization source `fa28628bb1e1a9ac507b99bb8b0002a039a3271cd75c6af494692732e33aa523`; offline test `7d41e42c12970cee40cbf756b4a9a8629fe01414006ac72232bcafbf1b90b56e`; collector `e49f3ee14d3ef0137f9be5a20c91b4a89281a42f204e79dad7d8c7a0e206b375`. The external `finalization-preuse-review.json` records the independent test/hash evidence. This is pre-use clearance of a bounded diagnostic, not a positive execution-completeness grade.

## F3 clarification — exact text versus negation polarity

### F3 development wording clarification — exact sentence corruption is not proven polarity loss

The 12 strict negation-sentence failures on four perspective-table development pages remain unchanged. A separate post-hoc context check found the authored core phrase “No medication change was recommended” in all 12/12 expected occurrences. Removing only OCR-inserted `|` glyphs in an offline sensitivity check restored all 12 complete sentence matches. Thus these cases show exact sentence corruption by table-rule artifacts; they do **not** establish that the negation word disappeared or that the statement's polarity flipped. No runtime output, primary scorer, acceptance threshold or selected arm was changed. The broader held-out result remains pending, and this diagnostic is awaiting independent verification.

## F6 preparation — matched PDF and image controls

### F6 pre-execution control refinement — rasterize the actual native PDF

Before generating or running the F6 corpus, the runner and independent preflight reviewer tightened the native-versus-image control. Every scored image will be a 300 dpi rendering of its actual native source PDF, including ruled graphics, followed by the already declared rotation. This replaces separately rendered nominally matching lines whose raster and PDF fonts had different metrics. Corpus size, authored line positions, split separation, arms, selection rules and acceptance criteria are unchanged.

The renderer is PDF.js `6.3.289` with bundled standard fonts and `useSystemFonts:false`; development PDFs use Helvetica and held-out PDFs Times-Roman. Host Verdana/Georgia fonts determine line wrapping only. Renderer and font hashes are included in the pending freeze. A separate two-page fictional mechanics check verified six native text lines exactly, both 2550×3300 rasters, visible text and ruled boxes, and nonblank crops. It caught and fixed a floating-point `ceil` producing a 3301-pixel height before any corpus image existed. This is a tooling check, not an F6 result or independent result verification. The full F6 experiment remains unexecuted.

Runtime source is 10,256 bytes.
- Quality reports use repeat 0 per page and timing uses all three repeats. CER is NFKC/whitespace-normalized, case-preserving sequence edit distance. Critical fields use occurrence counts. Paragraph omissions and reading-order measures use scorer-only truth alignment. Pure ordering attribution requires the same recognized word sequence for baseline/order and deskew/both pairs.
- Fixture parameters: letter pages at 2550×3300 pixels; LCG state = (1664525 × state + 1013904223) mod 2^32, seeded 41029 + 101 × document + 701 × family-name-length for development and 934771 for the held-out base. Six paragraphs use Marker/Panel Amber, Birch, Cedar, Dune, Elm and Fern; values (20 + state mod 700)/10; cyclic mmol/L, mg/dL and ng/mL; collection days 10–15 and month (document + 3); explicit no-medication/no-evidence statements, fictional context and print metadata. Development uses Arial 10 pt; heldout uses Times New Roman 10.5 pt. Columns change from two to three; table and annotation margins also change. Simulated notes use Bradley Hand 13 pt versus Comic Sans MS 12 pt. Positive/negative skew changes from 3/−3° to 2.5/−3.5°; perspective row strips widen from 86% to 100% versus 80% to 100%; blur changes from 1.5 to 1.8 px with JPEG quality 40 versus 35. All fixture strings and exact source/image hashes are retained with the generator.
- Runtime parameters for rebuilding the prototype: deskew uses 512-pixel-wide thumbnails, dark RGB sum <540 sampled every other x pixel, angles −4…4° in 0.25° steps and squared horizontal-projection counts; require a 1.015 score gain over zero and rotate the original by the negative inferred angle. Table guarding scans 512-pixel thumbnails at the same angles, y step 2, dark sum <650, at most two-pixel gaps, dark runs >45% width, distinct ruling separation >4 pixels and at least two rulings. Reconstruction groups actual TSV words by block/paragraph/line, retains all words, clusters x anchors within 8% page width, requires at least five lines and >10% vertical extent, and treats at least two major anchors separated by >18% width as columns unless table-guarded. Full-width lines exceed 70% width; isolated footer detection uses >72% page height, >6% vertical gap and at most three remaining lines. Remaining lines go to the nearest inferred anchor and sort by y/x. No per-page oracle choice is allowed.

Persistent-host full-batch outer GNU time: 2568.000 s CPU across720 jobs (3.567 s average), 2577.600 s elapsed, 583096 KiB maximum individual-process RSS. This includes worker startup and artifact/reporting overhead; per-job warm CPU times the preprocessing/reconstruction/OCR work and excludes final receipt serialization/persistence. The batch total does not assign that overhead to individual pages.

Development context clarification: the12 strict full negation-sentence failures on four perspective-table pages retain all12/12 authored “No medication change was recommended” prefixes. A separate post-hoc sensitivity check removing only inserted pipe glyphs restores all12 full sentences. This establishes table-rule corruption rather than a demonstrated polarity flip; the primary exact metric, outputs and thresholds remain unchanged.

**Supplemental regression critical-content diagnostic:**

- The frozen primary regression oracle had no critical labels. After the OCR run, a separate plan selected all generator-authored labeled date lines, Marker-A/B/C value-unit rows and the marginal note, before inspecting this supplemental field score. This post-hoc annotation does not change primary labels, selection or thresholds. The old fixtures contain no negation fact.
- baseline: 71/75 exact authored facts/notes retained; 0 introduced losses versus same-page baseline; 0 unknown comparisons.
- deskew: 71/75 exact authored facts/notes retained; 0 introduced losses versus same-page baseline; 0 unknown comparisons.
- order: 71/75 exact authored facts/notes retained; 0 introduced losses versus same-page baseline; 0 unknown comparisons.
- both: 71/75 exact authored facts/notes retained; 0 introduced losses versus same-page baseline; 0 unknown comparisons.
- Every arm retained 55/55 labeled dates and 15/15 value-unit rows, but only 1/5 complete marginal notes. This shared absolute loss remains visible even though no arm introduced a new loss versus baseline. Reproduce with `node f3-regression-critical.ts "$ARTIFACTS" plan` followed by `node f3-regression-critical.ts "$ARTIFACTS" score`; the separate plan pins the annotation rules, source truth hashes and scorer hash.

**Interpretation and reproducibility:**

- Held-out per-mode gates with the original quality/CPU/retention bars: baseline/cold FAIL; deskew/cold FAIL; order/cold FAIL; both/cold FAIL; baseline/warm FAIL; deskew/warm FAIL; order/warm FAIL; both/warm FAIL. A passing aggregate does not erase a failed page, stratum or timing repeat.
- Cold CPU is outer GNU time user+system for Node startup, preprocessing, reconstruction and child OCR. Persistent-host CPU sums per-job Node CPU plus child OCR CPU; CLI Tesseract is still a fresh process per page, so this is not a warmed OCR-engine comparison. Cold RSS is process-tree maximum individual-process high-water, not simultaneous total memory. Warm OCR RSS is per-page child high-water; warm Node RSS is the persistent worker lifetime high-water. Exact per-page medians/maxima and scopes are in scores.json.
- The primary regression critical comparison remains unmeasured; the separately labeled post-hoc diagnostic above supplies a bounded content check. Simulated handwriting fonts are not actual handwritten clinical pages. Fixture fonts and canvas version are recorded separately; all images and truth files were hash-frozen. Re-rendering with different host fonts creates different pixels. Every generated paragraph box fits within its page; maximum pre-transform bottom is 624.5 points.
- Reproduce with the supplied f3-generate.ts, f3-runtime.ts, f3-run.ts, f3-score.ts, f3-freeze.ts and Dockerfile.f3, repository Node dependencies and the T18 CLI base. Recreate the T18 fictional 300 dpi fixtures at the generator’s declared regression location, then generate a new external output root. Run development, score it, freeze source/data/image, run regression, run heldout cold then warm, and score both. The original freeze pins seven source/build files, 135 images and all oracles, eng traineddata and image ID. Use the retained images for byte-identical reruns.
- Commands from the prototype worktree: `node f3-generate.ts "$ARTIFACTS"`; `docker build -f Dockerfile.f3 -t circus-f3-ocr:experiment .`; `node f3-run.ts "$ARTIFACTS" development`; `node f3-score.ts "$ARTIFACTS" development`; `node f3-freeze.ts "$ARTIFACTS"`; `node f3-run.ts "$ARTIFACTS" regression`; `node f3-run.ts "$ARTIFACTS" holdout cold`; `node f3-run.ts "$ARTIFACTS" holdout warm`; `node f3-score.ts "$ARTIFACTS" regression`; `node f3-score.ts "$ARTIFACTS" holdout`. Raw images, OCR outputs, run logs, score caches, detailed scores, plans and freeze receipts remain outside Git.
- Formatting and focused TypeScript checks passed for the prototype and tooling. This entry is a runner report; no experiment checkbox is closed and no independent verification is claimed.

### F3 heldout failure-type clarification

The selected deskew arm and combined arm introduce four strict annotation failures, one on each positive-skew annotated page: the complete phrase `Please review prior values.` was present in baseline but no longer matched exactly after deskew. Ordering alone introduces eight strict collection-label/date failures on skewed column pages. The recognized word multiset remains identical in the pure-order comparisons; those ordering-only failures are sequence or association failures under the frozen literal criterion, not evidence of deleted OCR tokens. One inspected deskew example changes the note word `values` to `velues`; an exact-note failure should not be called an omitted whole note without inspecting its output. All original failures and criteria remain in force.

- deskew: introduced-loss pages 4.
- order: introduced-loss pages 8.
- both: introduced-loss pages 4.

## F2 results — second terminal primary cell

### F2 primary result 2 of 120 — first dense U10 repetition, 2026-09-25

Status: **two terminal primary receipts; one strictly complete receipt**. Cell `heldout-dense-0-r1-u10` finished with `complete: true` and `reading_exhausted`. The earlier U2 result remains strictly incomplete with `time_limit`; it is not relabeled. U15 is running in the unchanged primary schedule. This is one source and one repetition per observed arm, not the completed 120-cell comparison or evidence of general noninferiority.

The U10 run started at 04:55:54.638 UTC and ended after 2,548,859.487 ms (42.4810 minutes). Its unchanged 40-page dense source contains 160 expected observations and four fixed ten-page plan units. The literal grader returned 160/160 correct: recall 1, precision 1, zero missing, unexpected or mismatched records, zero duplicate groups/excess copies and zero wrong-person labels. Dense-source cross-page association and report-fact endpoints are not applicable. All 160 proposals nevertheless remain `subject: unknown`, identity `confirmation_required`, and unselectable. Literal success is distinct from established identity, acceptance readiness, or accepted data.

First literally correct proposal discovery occurred at 61,670.303 ms (61.6703 seconds), and discovery of at least 80/160 literal matches occurred at 1,272,897.406 ms (21.2150 minutes). These are polling observations with the same 100 ms-plus-scheduling/grading uncertainty as the prior cell. They do not measure identity-confirmed or selectable-record discovery.

The run made 122 actual model requests and reported 3,133,801 input + 71,145 output = **3,204,946 tokens**, including 1,045,376 cached-input tokens (33.3581% of input). Every successful request reported usage; all requests returned HTTP 200, with no recorded errors. The preregistered expected total was **3,796,283 tokens**, and the unchanged 2× boundary was **7,592,566 tokens**. Actual usage was 84.4233% of the expected estimate and 42.2116% of the stop boundary; no token overrun or explicit Resume extension occurred. This is reported token usage, not dollar cost.

Coverage and durability passed: exactly 40 one-page physical reads covering pages 1–40, no duplicate or missing page reads, four of four plan units accounted, no remaining units/pending windows, unchanged source SHA-256 and active plan/membership, and zero accepted document/observation/medication/procedure rows. Forty proposal publications were retained. The harness counted 22 bridge/model contexts; the application's cumulative job recorded **three bounded reading slices**, including two automatic productive-slice continuations. Those are distinct counters, and neither means 22 explicit user Resume operations. Explicit extensions were zero.

#### Why more requests accompanied fewer tokens in this particular pair

The raw response/tool receipts fully account for the difference:

- U2: 40 single-page read calls, 20 publication calls containing eight observations each, and two plan-read calls: **62 requests**. Every publication extracted one complete two-page unit. There were no separate no-tool completion responses; the two long model contexts ended at the reading deadline.
- U10: the same 40 single-page read calls, 40 publication calls containing four observations each, 22 plan-read calls, and 20 no-tool completion responses: **122 requests**. Thirty-six publication coverage entries said `inspected` for a still-partial ten-page unit; four said `extracted` when a unit was complete. There were no additional tool kinds and no extra physical source reads.

Thus the extra 60 requests are exactly 20 additional publications, 20 additional plan reads and 20 additional no-tool completion responses. A ten-page fixed plan unit did **not** force a ten-page publication. In this run, the model published one page at a time, often ended its turn after a short amount of progress, and resumed in a fresh model context. The two-page run published two pages at a time and retained much longer contexts.

The request payloads demonstrate the tradeoff: mean reported input fell from 64,221.823 tokens/request at U2 to 25,686.893 at U10; maximum input fell from 116,524 to 37,177. Summed textual request characters fell from 15,173,847 to 12,455,205, and maximum textual request size fell from 427,653 to 151,609 characters. Both runs sent 205,056 media bytes across their requests. U10's more frequent fresh contexts contained less accumulated transcript, which is consistent with its lower total input despite more requests. This is an explanation supported by the observed request/context traces; it does not isolate unit size from the model strategy that changed alongside it.

Descriptively, U10 used **20.6408% fewer total reported tokens** than U2 (21.2959% fewer input tokens but 25.3038% more output tokens) while making **96.7742% more requests**. Its terminal elapsed time was **30.1228% longer**. The first literal proposal appeared 36.9609% sooner because a smaller publication appeared early, while reaching half the expected literal proposals took 30.8615% longer. These single-pair numbers are exploratory; the U2 terminal reason also differs, so they are not a successful paired-completion or noninferiority comparison.

This live behavior limits the inference from F1's scripted throughput assumption: F1 deliberately submitted one publication per fixed unit. The live model chose different publication sizes and turn boundaries, so F1's call-count reduction is not an enforced property of the current host. Preserve these strategy changes as measured outcomes, rather than changing prompts, forcing publication sizes, or claiming that unit size alone caused the observed token difference. No heldout source, grading rule or active runtime was modified.

Receipt: `runs/heldout-dense-0-r1-u10/result.json`, SHA-256 `4d7e2f0bd115058ef4c232aa2b287c862da6ec2ea7f9181e2892203442ae938f`. The runner reconstructed the descriptive tool/context counts with external TypeScript `compare-first-dense.ts`; its saved output is `first-dense-comparison.json` in the artifact root. Reproduction command: `node compare-first-dense.ts ARTIFACT_ROOT`. Raw request/response and tool/read/publication receipts remain under each original cell. A separate agent must independently verify this new terminal result and comparison; this runner entry is not self-verification.

## Independent verification — completed F3 held-out matrix

### Verification 2026-09-25 — independent verifier, F3 heldout matrix and reduced OCR reproduction

**Agree with the recorded unsuccessful qualification.** Independently re-derived all 1,440 heldout receipts: 60 pages × four arms × three repetitions × cold/warm modes, all with successful process status. Verified the frozen seven source files, plan and manifest, and all 135 input/oracle pairs; independently calculated sequence-sensitive edit distances, exact critical-field occurrence counts, paragraph omissions/order, logical block order, timing summaries, and memory scopes from saved outputs and raw timing/TSV receipts. Reconciled all 128 group summaries and all 240 detailed stratum lines. Every page/arm produced the same final text across its six timing runs. Order-only transformations preserved the recognized word multiset and matched the paired baseline/deskew word hashes.

For baseline, deskew, order, and both respectively, re-derived edits were 18,915 / 5,144 / 15,753 / 1,144 against 170,025 characters: CER 11.124835% / 3.025437% / 9.265108% / 0.672842%. Pages exceeding 3% CER were 16 / 4 / 12 / 0. Exact critical occurrences retained were 1,094 / 1,292 / 1,088 / 1,292 of 1,340; introduced-loss page counts were 0 / 4 / 8 / 4. Paragraph omissions were 24 / 0 / 24 / 0 of 360, paragraph inversions 26 / 20 / 18 / 0, and logical block inversions 37 / 20 / 19 / 0. The selected deskew arm therefore fails the frozen heldout criteria, including aggregate CER, four perspective-column pages with approximately 29.94–29.95% CER, and four introduced exact annotation errors. These column failures concern ordering; deskew omitted no scored paragraphs.

Cold CPU maxima were 2.90 / 3.83 / 6.03 / 6.68 seconds, with 0 / 0 / 31 / 170 of 180 runs exceeding five seconds. Warm maxima were 2.550386 / 2.970419 / 5.304165 / 5.834851 seconds, with 0 / 0 / 11 / 153 of 180 exceeding five seconds. Thus the combined arm's stronger CER does not qualify it: its CPU and introduced-field-loss criteria fail. Warm means a persistent Node worker with a fresh OCR child for each page. Cold timing includes the in-container runtime and OCR CPU, not Docker launch latency. Cold RSS is the maximum individual process high-water, not concurrent process-tree memory; warm host high-water is lifetime/shared across arms and does not establish an arm-specific memory cost.

**Exact-loss interpretation checked independently.** On each of the four positive-skew annotated heldout pages, baseline contains “Please review prior values.” and deskew contains “Please review prior velues.” This is a transcription error that fails the exact-field criterion; it is not evidence that the whole note disappeared. Order's eight introduced `Collected: YYYY-MM-DD` failures occur with unchanged recognized-word hashes, so they concern sequence/association rather than deleted OCR words. Separately, the four development perspective-table pages retain all 12 occurrences of the core phrase “No medication change was recommended.” Removing only inserted pipe glyphs offline restores all 12 full-sentence matches. This post-hoc sensitivity check supports table-rule corruption, not a claim that negation was lost or polarity reversed. The original 12 strict sentence failures remain unchanged.

**Reduced real OCR reproduction also agrees.** Before F6 timing began, independently reran all four arms on one heldout perspective-table page, one perspective-column page, and one positive-skew annotated failure page using the frozen image `sha256:8d784a58d02c25943b7c7b785d557b94827c493d22b6d29ee1f2dc0d511ed568`, one CPU, 2 GiB memory, no network, and `OMP_THREAD_LIMIT=1`. All 12 runs exactly reproduced final-text hashes, full word/box lists, and deskew/reconstruction metadata from the retained cold first repetition. These are output-reproduction checks; their timings are not added to or substituted into the original matrix. OCR execution was then released to the F6 runner.

For a reduced actual rerun, use the immutable image/configuration above and the declared perspective-table, perspective-column, and positive-skew annotated fixtures, retaining fresh outputs separately. The detailed stratum report makes every passing and failing family/distortion group reviewable.

The 60-page split contains 12 underlying fictional documents across five distortions, not 60 independent document templates. Fonts and rasterization are frozen fixture dependencies; this does not qualify natural handwriting, arbitrary scanners, clinical deployment, or a production OCR replacement. The primary regression critical-field endpoint remains unmeasured; its separate supplemental labels and the negation sensitivity analysis remain post-hoc.
