**Decision and reproducibility:**

- Eligible native unchanged gate: FAIL. Experimental image usable-index gate: FAIL. Known-span containment alone is insufficient to qualify either decision. Zero observed misses on 20 held-out documents is screening evidence, not a deployment safety guarantee.
- Confidence and unassigned ink are imperfect quality signals, not evidence that an original page is empty or safe to hide. Acceptance filters an experimental index only. Originals and unqualified OCR remain available to the ordinary reading path.
- Reproduce with f6-generate.ts, f6-freeze-inputs.ts, f6-run.ts, f6-index.ts, f6-features.ts, f6-score.ts, f6-freeze.ts and the copied frozen fingerprint/date/PDF writer modules. Supply the F3 pinned Docker image and development-frozen OCR selection. Run generation to an external artifact root, then development OCR, features, index and scoring; freeze the one selected risk point plus all sources/inputs/oracles; then run the same held-out stages unchanged. Source manifest separates opaque runtime files from the scorer-only partner map and oracle. Each OCR invocation mounts images/jobs read-only and outputs writable, without the oracle.
- Commands from the isolated prototype worktree: `node f6-generate.ts "$ARTIFACTS"`; `node f6-freeze-inputs.ts "$ARTIFACTS"`; `node f6-run.ts "$ARTIFACTS" development`; `node f6-features.ts "$ARTIFACTS" development`; `node f6-index.ts "$ARTIFACTS" development`; `node f6-score.ts "$ARTIFACTS" development`; `node f6-freeze.ts "$ARTIFACTS"`; then the same run/features/index/score commands with `holdout`. The frozen runtime/image, OCR selection, fingerprint-module hashes, font environment, generated source files, raw OCR/PDF extraction outputs, all grid points and per-document mapping/edit receipts remain outside Git.
- Repository format and typecheck commands and focused prototype TypeScript checks passed. No Git commit, merge, push, production change, experiment checkbox closure or self-verification was performed. Independent result verification remains required.

### F6 final findings and interpretation clarification — 2026-09-25

**Runner measurements are complete; independent verification is pending. The combined pipeline fails the original gates.** All 320 development and 320 heldout OCR calls completed successfully, followed by the full six-arm indexing/scoring matrix. A final runner integrity check found all 14 frozen source hashes and 492 frozen input hashes unchanged. The final report already contains every predeclared heldout risk-grid point and all 30 arm/family outcomes; no additional subset was selected.

On the 20 heldout document pairs, the experimental native-text matcher reduces false unchanged decisions from 32 to zero while retaining 112 true unchanged decisions. Its precision is therefore 100%, but unchanged recall remains 112/140 = 80%, below the 95% bar. Both native matchers recover zero of 36 complete passages in the within-passage column family; that failure exists without OCR.

With deskew and the experimental matcher, automatic passage recovery is 120/180 = 66.67%, correct candidate identity is 99/160 = 61.88%, and related-passage recall is 96/160 = 60%. The scorer-assisted known-span diagnostic recovers 156/180 = 86.67%, also below 90%; it does not repair automatic segmentation or identify the correct earlier passage. Complete new-content regions survive in 16/20 documents, but this prototype does not implement a separate novelty classifier or user retrieval interface.

The development-frozen confidence/ink point accepts 16/20 heldout experimental indexes. Only 4/20 documents have usable accepted indexes; 12/16 accepted indexes fail strict critical-edit recovery. Thus accepted coverage of 80% is not usable coverage of 80%: usable coverage is 20%, and conditional strict-critical-recovery failure is 75%. No threshold was changed after seeing heldout outcomes. The same poor result is retained across the predeclared grid; none of those alternatives establishes the required useful-index outcome.

**Strict edit-recovery failure is not synonymous with loss of the literal field in OCR.** In the deskew arm, all 20 old critical fields, all 16 non-deletion new critical fields and all four expected deleted-fact absences survive in their own passages. Only four corrections also receive the correct earlier-passage association, changed label and required localized edits. The experiment therefore exposes segmentation, association and change-localization failures beyond character recognition. Its `hiddenCriticalCorrections` counter means accepted indexes missing that strict recovery result; it does not mean an original page was hidden or removed. All originals remain visible and all re-exports are read in full.

The final report's sentence beginning “Zero observed misses on 20 held-out documents” is a **hypothetical small-sample limitation**, not a claim about current whole-pipeline performance. This run has 12 strict recovery failures among 16 accepted experimental indexes. Zero observed native false-unchanged decisions is a separate, narrowly measured outcome and does not erase those failures. Even a future zero-miss sample of this size would remain screening evidence rather than a deployment safety guarantee.

Native and deskew date-role recovery is 223/260, with 37 extra/wrong roles. Zero false date exclusions is measured only on this all-newer-than-cutoff corpus; old-only exclusion selectivity remains unknown. Family and correction type are confounded, so the family outcomes do not establish which edit type is intrinsically harder. F3 component failures and the original F6 adoption prerequisite's failed/deferred status remain in force. No product default, threshold, result or frozen source was changed.

## Independent verification — complete F6 diagnostic matrix

### Verification 2026-09-25 — independent verifier, complete F6 development and heldout outcomes

**Agree with the reported failed qualification.** Both splits were independently reproduced in an external artifact directory using the frozen sources: fresh extraction from all 80 actual native PDFs, reconstruction of the OCR-derived items, all six matcher/index arms, confidence and dark-pixel features from the images, the full scorers, and development risk-point selection. The resulting pipeline, feature and score objects match the original objects exactly for both splits. All 14 frozen source hashes and 492 frozen input hashes remain unchanged; the final freeze is `ab2f72993c258c60ebeab6600c41c2953700df5999a6082112fedcc80f40d49b`. Its timestamp precedes the first heldout run log. Verified 80 distinct PDF hashes and 320 distinct image hashes, not merely unique filenames.

Separately re-derived the 640 successful OCR calls from both ordered run logs and raw TSV receipts, including each recognized word, confidence and box, and checked the alternating baseline/experimental schedule and image hashes. Independently recalculated literal LCS coverage/purity for all 1,238 matched regions across the twelve split/arm results, checked one-to-one mappings and retained missing/extra regions, and reconciled per-document candidate, related, unchanged, new-content, strict-edit, date, literal-critical-retention and usefulness aggregates. Reconstructed all 160 development/heldout risk-grid rows directly from document outcomes and independently rebuilt the fixed development ranking. No development target point has zero accepted strict-critical-recovery misses; the declared fallback selects confidence 90 and maximum unassigned ink 0.2. The heldout stage applies that point unchanged and does not select an alternative from its grid.

**Reduced actual OCR also reproduces.** Independently ran both OCR variants on the changed-passage page of the first development pair in families 0 and 3 and the first heldout pair in families 1, 3 and 4. All ten fresh calls reproduce final-text hashes, full recognized word/box lists and deskew/reconstruction metadata exactly. The immutable image was `sha256:8d784a58d02c25943b7c7b785d557b94827c493d22b6d29ee1f2dc0d511ed568`, with one CPU, 2 GiB memory, no network and `OMP_THREAD_LIMIT=1`. These separate calls verify outputs; their timings do not extend or replace the original matrix. No provider requests or frozen artifact writes were needed.

The measured failures are reproducible beyond OCR quality alone. In each split the native experimental matcher has unchanged TP/FP/FN 112/0/28: observed precision 100%, recall 80%, below the 95% recall criterion. Its native baseline counterpart has 112/32/28 and four unchanged decisions overlapping changed content. Both native controls recover zero of the 36 complete passages in the within-passage column family; the extra-region count includes metadata as declared, so it is not a count solely of spurious clinical passages. Exact image-sameness is deliberately ineligible in every image arm; zero image unchanged decisions must not be represented as successful native unchanged recall.

For experimental OCR plus experimental matching, development automatic segmentation is 88/180, correct candidate identity 75/160, related recall 74/160, complete new-content regions 8/20 and strict localized corrections 4/20. All 20 documents pass the selected quality signal, but only four are usable, with 16 accepted strict-critical-recovery misses. Heldout segmentation is 120/180, identity 99/160, related recall 96/160, new-content regions 16/20 and strict corrections 4/20. The heldout quality point accepts 16/20, but usable accepted coverage remains 4/20; 12 of those 16 accepted indexes fail strict recovery, a conditional failure rate of 75%. It therefore fails both the 80% usable-index gate and the zero-miss bar. All four heldout image-arm selected-point outcomes and every reported grid point reconcile; there is no hidden qualifying point in the declared grid.

**Retain the endpoint distinctions.** Experimental known-span containment is 180/180 on development and 156/180 on heldout. It uses authored spans in scoring and cannot establish automatic segmentation, candidate identity, precision or usability. New-content coverage means an automatically segmented region survives, not that an implemented novelty classifier or user interface identifies it as new. Deskew/native literal critical retention is 20/20 old fields, 16/16 non-deletion new fields and four of four deleted-fact absences in both splits. Yet only four corrections meet the stricter correct-reference, changed-label and localized-edit requirement. The accepted `hiddenCriticalCorrections` count is consequently a failure to recover the change in the index, not proof that the literal critical text vanished, an original was hidden, or a clinical record was accepted. Inspected examples include uncertain/wrong candidate selection despite complete native text, and fragmented passages despite retained critical fields. The report's separate clarification of its hypothetical “zero observed misses” sentence is necessary and accurate: this heldout pipeline actually has 12 accepted strict-recovery failures.

Date-role results also reproduce: native/deskew 161/260 with 99 extra/wrong roles on development and 223/260 with 37 extra/wrong roles on heldout; baseline OCR 130/260 and 151/260 respectively. All governing dates in the authored corpus are newer than the cutoff. Zero false exclusions therefore says nothing about old-only selectivity. The scorer's expected date roles come from authored oracle labels, not parser output. Runtime indexing and image quality signals use opaque source files and the earlier-import index; scoring-only partner IDs, truth boxes, edit locations and generation angles are not runtime inputs.

Portable reproduction: preserve the frozen inputs and hashes; create a fresh external output root with read-only access to those inputs/receipts; run `f6-index.ts`, `f6-features.ts` and `f6-score.ts` for development, then heldout, and compare parsed output objects with the retained receipts. Check each run log against the corresponding TSV and image hash; independently sum document counters and reconstruct every confidence/ink acceptance set and development ranking. For the reduced OCR check, use the ten declared family/page/variant cases and pinned container configuration above with fresh writable output paths. The already recorded generator, manifests, component hashes and full family/grid results provide the reconstruction parameters.

This completes independent verification of F6's declared diagnostic execution, with failed scientific/adoption gates. Five template families per split and four related examples per family are bounded synthetic coverage; correction type is confounded with family. The scorer's geometric and known-span assistance, fixed raster/font environment, one timing repetition, lack of old-only date selectivity and lack of a novelty/retrieval interface remain limitations. F3's failures and F6's original adoption-prerequisite failure/deferment remain intact; no threshold or product default is qualified. An overall positive execution-completeness grade is still withheld while F2's preregistered matrix remains incomplete.

## F2 results — fifteen-page completion and preserved twenty-page interruption

#### F2 runner result: first dense U15 cell, 2026-09-25

This is the third terminal receipt in the frozen 120-cell primary matrix, for `heldout-dense-0-r1-u15` (order 2, repetition 1). It is one fictional 40-page dense document with 160 expected observations. The unchanged route, grading rules, model settings, fixed plan, and 2× token guard apply. This entry preserves the earlier U2 incomplete and U10 complete outcomes; it does not establish an arm-wide result.

The U15 run completed with `reading_exhausted`, all 3 plan units accounted, 40/40 physical pages read exactly once, zero pending read windows and zero remaining units. All 160 expected literal observations matched: precision 1.0, recall 1.0, zero missing observations, duplicates, unexpected records or wrong-person associations under the frozen grader. These are literal proposal matches. All 160 proposals still have unknown subject, require identity confirmation, and are unselectable; zero identities were established and zero clinical records were accepted. Cross-page association and single-report fact criteria do not apply to this dense fixture. Full narrative critical-error auditing and all remaining matrix cells remain separate requirements.

The observed duration was 2,498,643.7085 ms (41.6441 minutes), starting at 2026-09-25 05:38:23.616 UTC. First literally correct proposal discovery was 60,044.028834 ms; discovery of at least half the expected literal matches was 1,247,906.981792 ms (20.7984 minutes). These are polling observations with approximately 100 ms observation granularity plus scheduling delay, not times to an identity-confirmed or selectable record.

There were 116 provider requests across 19 model contexts and 3 ordinary bounded reading sessions, with no explicit Resume. Response reconstruction gives 19 plan calls, 40 one-page read calls, 40 publications of four clinical records each, and 17 responses without tool calls. Of the 40 publication coverage entries, 37 were `inspected` partial-unit entries and 3 were `extracted` completed-unit entries. A 15-page plan unit therefore did not force a single publication or a single model context per unit. The 40 one-page reads and four-record publications match the earlier U10 strategy, while U15 used 3 fewer plan calls/model contexts and 3 fewer no-tool completions. These observed model strategies are not a controlled causal isolation of unit size.

All 116 responses supplied usage. Summing them yields 3,082,739 input tokens plus 69,996 output tokens = **3,152,735 total tokens**, including 988,800 cached input tokens (already part of input, not additional). The frozen expected cost was 3,942,232 tokens; the 2× stop was 7,884,464. Actual usage was 79.9734% of expected and 39.9867% of the stop. No overrun, external error, source mutation, plan-membership change or accepted write was recorded. The retained chat still equals its terminal journal snapshot; its checkpoint records all 40 seen windows, zero pending windows, and 3 completed/accounted units.

Descriptively, against the same fixture's preceding U10 repetition, U15 used 1.6291% fewer tokens, 4.9180% fewer requests and 1.9701% less elapsed time. #### F2 runner interruption: first dense U20 cell, 2026-09-25

The fourth original terminal receipt, `heldout-dense-0-r1-u20` (order 3, repetition 1), records an external quota failure, not completion. The first four requests returned HTTP 200. Request 5 returned HTTP 429 after 1,134.240417 ms with nested provider error type `usage_limit_reached`, message “The usage limit has been reached,” and reset timestamp 1790656980 (2026-09-29 04:43:00 UTC). The response has no usage object, model result, or Retry-After/rate-limit headers. That historical reset field is not proof of current route availability.

The cell began at 2026-09-25 06:20:02.369 UTC and produced a terminal receipt after 63,420.358375 ms. It retained one publication containing 4/160 expected literal observations (recall .025, precision 1.0, 156 missing), with first literal discovery at 57,909.454625 ms. Half-completion was not reached. All four retained records require identity confirmation and are unselectable. Zero accepted clinical writes, original-hash changes, plan-membership changes or duplicate physical reads were recorded.

The exact successful response totals are 62,590 input + 1,757 output = **64,347 measured tokens**, including 28,800 cached input tokens. Request totals were 10,947, 12,343, 19,666 and 21,391 tokens. The fifth request has unknown usage; 64,347 is a lower bound, not a complete cost. The receipt's `allSuccessfulRequestsReported=true` describes only successful requests. The final reading projection correctly reports `modelUsageIncomplete=true`. The underlying retained checkpoint has a legacy boolean `modelUsageIncomplete=false`, but also `unmeasuredRequests=1` and attribution totals of 5 attempts, 4 responses, 1 failure and 1 unknown-usage attempt. These explicit counters prevent treating the legacy boolean as evidence of zero usage.

Two host PDF reads returned original pages 1 and 2, while the durable checkpoint acknowledges only page 1 and retains page 2 as a pending read window. Page 2's result was prepared before the failed request; it was not acknowledged by a successful subsequent model response. Both 20-page units remain unaccounted. The terminal batch is paused with `model_unavailable`, and the reading state is paused with `error`. The retained chat equals its terminal journal snapshot. Nothing was restarted, reset, accepted or discarded.

The frozen modeled cost is 3,796,283 tokens and the unchanged 2× stop is 7,592,566. The previously reviewed continuation protocol expressly requires reconciliation of failed or aborted request usage before continuing. The unchanged collector now classifies this cell `budget_unresolved`; `resumeCell` rejects any missing request usage. An ordinary same-run continuation is therefore **not currently eligible**, regardless of whether quota has subsequently become available. No model probe, live Resume, new profile, new token allowance, quota reset, skipped arm or source/runtime modification was performed during this inspection.

The primary process has exited. Four of 120 cells have original terminal receipts: U2 is terminal incomplete, U10 and U15 are strictly complete, and U20 is unresolved. The remaining 116 cells are unstarted. The ordered controller must resume the earliest interrupted eligible cell before dispatching a later arm. Full matrix and finalization diagnostics remain unfinished. The original U20 result and all earlier outcomes are preserved.

A separate independent investigation is checking whether historical provider/proxy evidence can establish exact usage or a rigorously conservative finite bound for request 5. The existing recorded error alone does not establish either. Any interval-based accounting extension would require a separately declared and independently reviewed protocol, preserve unknown measured usage and immutable original receipts, charge a conservative bound against the same cap, retain all later outcomes, and keep runtime/source/checkpoint continuity. No such extension is approved or implemented by this entry.

### Independent read-only U20 accounting investigation — 2026-09-25

**Conclusion: unresolved.** The retained rejection and installed local route code establish neither exact rejected-request usage nor a rigorously conservative finite token bound suitable for debiting this request under the original cell cap. No provider call, authentication readout, live continuation, source/protocol edit or zero-usage assumption was made.

The immutable plan's `heldout-dense-0-r1-u20` cell has modeled tokens **3,796,283** and stop tokens **7,592,566**. The first four successful responses report 62,590 input plus 1,757 output tokens = **64,347** total. Request 5 reports no usage. The arithmetic difference, **7,528,219**, is remaining cap headroom; it is not an upper bound on the unmeasured request.

Evidence inspected:

- The retained fifth request has keys `model`, `messages`, `tools`, `tool_choice`, `stream`, and `disable_fallbacks`. It has no `max_tokens`, `max_output_tokens` or `max_completion_tokens`. Its recorded 121,588 text characters and 4,272 media bytes are payload-size measurements, not provider token accounting.
- The fifth saved response is an HTTP 429 error with upstream type `usage_limit_reached`. It contains quota-reset metadata but no usage object; the retained rate-header map is empty. HTTP status, short duration and absence of returned model output do not establish zero input, reasoning or output usage.
- In `src/scripts/experiment-f2-run.ts`, lines 45–104, `outputCapTokens` participates in the structural estimator's publication-batch calculation. It is not a transmitted limit in the saved request. Lines 419–442 count only finite provider-reported prompt/completion token fields and check the cumulative cell cap after a response. A timeout or cumulative post-response check cannot prove a per-request token maximum.
- The live experimental proxy is the existing pinned LiteLLM 1.99.1 image with the reviewed compatibility patches. Its installed `litellm/llms/chatgpt/responses/transformation.py`, `transform_responses_api_request`, adds default instructions, forces provider streaming, requests encrypted reasoning content, and returns a whitelist of `model`, `input`, `instructions`, `stream`, `store`, `include`, `tools`, `tool_choice`, `reasoning`, `previous_response_id`, and `truncation`. All max-token fields are absent from that whitelist. Therefore the estimator's 16,384 output-token assumption cannot be treated as an enforced provider request limit.
- Installed `litellm/llms/custom_httpx/http_handler.py`, `AsyncHTTPHandler.post`, calls `response.raise_for_status()` and raises a masked status error for HTTP failure. `litellm/llms/custom_httpx/llm_http_handler.py`, `async_response_api_handler` / `_handle_error`, propagates that failure before successful response transformation. This explains why local successful-response usage conversion cannot recover a counter from this error; it says nothing authoritative about upstream usage before the rejection.
- The saved request and reviewed route do not establish provider-side media expansion/tokenization limits, an enforced total input-plus-output maximum, or billing semantics for this quota rejection. Payload lengths, earlier empirical PDF calibration, modeled context/output allowances and a current account-availability snapshot are insufficient substitutes. No package metadata estimate was promoted to a provider-enforced ceiling.
- `src/scripts/experiment-f2-control.ts:95` collects requests missing usage; lines 105–111 mark their known totals as a lower bound and select `budget_unresolved`. `experiment-f2-continue.ts:301` explicitly refuses continuation while any previous request lacks usage. ## F2 supplemental reconstruction — estimated request size, actual charge unknown

### Supplemental U20 rejected-request input-size reconstruction — 2026-09-25

**About 27,500 input tokens is a defensible empirical estimate for the saved request's input size; it is not reconstructed billed usage.** The frozen structural model estimates 32,377 input tokens. Actual charged input, output/reasoning and total usage for the rejected request remain unknown. No number below was substituted into the original usage ledger, and the separately owner-approved accounting exception is a policy decision rather than a recovered measurement.

The saved fifth request contains exactly 121,588 transcript text characters under the frozen `proxyTranscriptSize` function and two embedded, distinct, one-page PDFs. Their data URLs occupy 4,272 encoded bytes; the decoded PDFs total 3,160 bytes. These sizes and page counts are reconstructable exactly. The provider's transformed/tokenized input is not recorded exactly.

The frozen cost-model calculation is `121588 / 4 + 1624 + 2 × 178 = 32377` input tokens: four characters per token, the calibrated adapter prefix, and the predeclared 178-token allowance per PDF page. The original two matched PDF calibration probes measured additive input of 178 and 74 tokens; 178 was selected for modeling. Neither that two-probe maximum nor the character ratio is a hard bound.

The nearest completed unit arms on the same document, repetition and request position supply closer empirical comparisons, selected without looking at their quality grades:

- U15 request 5: 122,180 text characters, the same encoded-media byte count, and 27,668 measured input tokens. Applying the frozen character conversion only to the 592-character difference gives **27,520** estimated input tokens for U20.
- U10 request 5: 122,643 text characters, the same encoded-media byte count, and 27,913 measured input tokens. Correcting the 1,055-character difference gives **27,649.25**.
- Within U20, request 2→3 adds the first PDF/read result: 28,166 characters and 5,839 measured input tokens. Request 4→5 adds 29,305 characters and another PDF. Anchoring at request 4's 21,326 input tokens gives `21326 + 5839 + (29305 − 28166)/4 =` **27,449.75**.

These three retrospective estimates span roughly **27,450–27,650 input tokens**. That is a spread of estimates, not a confidence interval or guaranteed range: content composition, media processing, adapter framing and provider tokenization can differ. U10/U15 request 5 returned 1,553/1,519 output tokens respectively, but those successful outputs do not establish how much output or reasoning the rejected U20 request generated or was charged for. The rejection still has no usage counter. Token estimates also do not establish a currency charge.

The original known subtotal remains **64,347 tokens** under the unchanged **7,592,566-token** cap. This supplemental calculation does not turn either input estimate into actual rejected-request usage or prove a cap upper bound.

Reproduce offline with Node 24 and the frozen worktree dependencies: `node reconstruct.ts "$ARTIFACTS" "$F2_WORKTREE" "$EXTERNAL_OUTPUT/estimate.json"`. The TypeScript script reads the original plan, receipts, PDF calibration and saved request, recomputes transcript size, parses only the embedded fictional PDFs' page counts, and emits source hashes plus all formulas and comparators. It makes no provider call and does not read authentication material or change originals. The supplemental `estimate.json` is separate from all experiment accounting receipts.

### Independent verification — rejected-request input estimate

The coordinator, independent of the reconstruction author, re-derived the arithmetic directly from original request and response files. Removing embedded data URLs from a cloned request gives 121,588 serialized text characters; the two decoded fictional PDFs contain two page objects and total 3,160 bytes, versus 4,272 encoded data-URL bytes. Checked the comparator input counts against their raw provider responses, then independently recomputed 27,649.25 and 27,520 from U10/U15 and 27,449.75 from the within-U20 read-step extrapolation. The frozen formula yields 32,377. All reported arithmetic agrees.

This verifies an input-size estimate and its reproducible inputs, not actual rejected-request consumption. The successful comparator outputs cannot recover missing reasoning/output usage. The reported spread remains a set of retrospective estimates, not a confidence interval or proven bound. Original usage remains null and the known cumulative subtotal remains 64,347. No provider calls, numeric accounting substitution or original receipt changes occurred.

#### Independent verification — F2 mixed0 repetition 1, all four arms, 2026-09-25

**Agree with all four `no_progress` failures; flag a host-read counting correction and a reproducible context-retention limitation.** Each original primary cell was attempted under the frozen protocol and stopped with zero returned records, zero publications, 0/24 literal recall and **0/24 person/date association recall**. Precision is undefined with no output. Unlike the dense fixtures, this mixed fixture has association denominator 24; boilerplate about a zero denominator must not be interpreted as making this endpoint unmeasured. No short failure duration is successful processing speed.

I independently hash-pinned each receipt, checked every response, rederived usage with the reviewed collector, and read the actual source, chat checkpoint, batch/intake journal and read-only database/feed. Exact source hash is unchanged at `7aa7163f7a82889b6b7bfeb360aa4f8bc7f189a9da4f3e7c7f1628961f2a0f5e`. Fixed-plan identity/membership is unchanged in each cell. Actual accepted documents, observations, medications and procedures are each zero, with zero retained proposal records. The four outcomes are separately preserved:

- **U15:** 272,351 input + 662 output = **273,013 tokens**, including 98,560 cached input; 54,208.357125 ms to failure. Receipt SHA-256 `3b6c946be159ab7032109aa70749a29057065c6bd913f47d75de8f38e39392b1`.
- **U20:** 272,721 input + 701 output = **273,422 tokens**, including 96,000 cached input; 55,907.751000 ms. Receipt SHA-256 `82965dca3ee9f53f66f6ed1ba60190f4c2c150ad35fbf1e09799304469ef691c`.
- **U2:** 274,465 input + 729 output = **275,194 tokens**, including 105,600 cached input; 54,853.087958 ms. Receipt SHA-256 `e98e01f1bfbfc8dfd15418b4f0c00b9fafa08785ec73410654df65fb4d7953d5`.
- **U10:** 273,535 input + 706 output = **274,241 tokens**, including 96,000 cached input; 56,825.865042 ms. Receipt SHA-256 `56a04293227bd5a728946bebb2839136a1d6f400a24cbece37d730bc67e02ce7`.

Each has **11 HTTP-200 responses with fully reported input/output/cache usage**, one model context and one budgeted slice. All totals are below their frozen token thresholds. The responses request one plan read followed by ten page reads: `1,2,3,1,2,3,1,2,3,1`. There are no model batch publications and no prose completion responses; every response's content is null. Thus these are application/model progress failures, not quota or usage-accounting failures. Literal discovery and identity/selectability milestones were never reached. Zero wrong-person output does not compensate for failure to extract any of the 24 expected observations.

**Counting correction:** the frozen harness's nine `physicalPageReads` entries are nine successfully returned read receipts, sequence `1,2,3` repeated three times. Actual durable attribution independently records **ten host reads**: page 1 four times and pages 2/3 three times each; acknowledged counts are three per page. Therefore each cell has **three unique host-read pages, seven host rereads, nine acknowledged reads and six duplicate acknowledged receipts**, with 37 pages never read. The tenth underlying page-1 read succeeds on the host, then the no-progress guard throws before its result is exposed to the model. This follows the frozen `assistant.ts` order: tool execution, attribution, then repeated-read guard. The earlier wording that the read was rejected before it happened, or that nine receipts count all physical host work, is too strong. Keep the original fields unchanged and interpret them with this correction.

**Mechanism evidence, not causal proof:** actual PDF page 1 supplies patient identity and encounter date; page 3 supplies three results and explicitly refers identity/date to the section opening page. In every arm, request 5 retains literal pages 2 and 3, while page 1's text/schema and PDF evidence have become `HOST_TRANSCRIPT_RECEIPT` entries. Its patient/date literals are absent from the request. Subsequent requests cycle exact page pairs `[3,1]`, `[1,2]`, `[2,3]` as the model requests the missing third page, never publishing. This agrees with frozen `proxy-transcript.ts`: one consumed read remains exact alongside the current read, and receipts direct rereading when text is needed. The observed model behavior plus a three-page encounter and two-page exact retention support a context-retention failure hypothesis. They do not establish the model's internal reason or prove that increasing retention would repair extraction; that requires a separately declared diagnostic. No prompt, runtime, guard, source or primary outcome was changed during verification.

External reconstruction evidence is under `mixed0-failures/` (U15/U20/U2) and `mixed0-u10-failure/`, with per-cell hash-pinned manifests/collector outputs and actual-state `verification.json`. Independent scripts `verify-mixed0-failures.mts` and `verify-mixed0-u10.mts` compare actual source/state, read all responses, inspect every request's exact/evicted page list and reconcile durable host/acknowledged counts. No verifier provider call or Git write occurred. These are fully attempted failed primary cells; overall execution completeness and final comparative claims remain pending the remaining planned runs.

#### Independent verification — F2 heldout-mixed-1-r1-u20, 2026-09-25

**Agree with the failed primary outcome, with more precise provenance and read-count interpretations.** The run stops for `no_progress`, returns 21 observations, qualifies 18/24 under the frozen literal-plus-provenance grader (recall 0.75, precision 18/21 = 0.857143), and passes 0/24 required person/date associations. It is not a completed processing job despite reading every unique page.

I independently hash-pinned terminal SHA-256 `4194ba99ee68fc40f85828cd3094aaee6ef45a6329cb2d85ae540c558ff3023a`, validated frozen source/artifact and plan hashes, rederived usage/quality, and inspected actual source bytes, all 21 durable proposal files, chat checkpoint, batch/intake state and the read-only database/feed. Source hash remains `86250aba2267034a2298a288ef3e6c429e05f81e9450a437434185fd7acca26f`; plan identity and its two units are unchanged. Actual proposals and 21 records equal the receipt, with zero accepted documents, observations, medications and procedures. All returned records require identity confirmation, retain unknown subject and are unselectable.

**Provenance interpretation:** expected answers 18–20 contain the correct page-37 values/units and the correct January 17 encounter date from page 31. Their raw locators explicitly say `original page 37 …; encounter date page 31`. The frozen exact-page parser rejects multiple page references in one locator, so these three records fail the required single-page provenance check. This is a real frozen endpoint failure, not evidence that the transcribed values or encounter date are wrong. Expected answers 21–23, the three January 18 page-40 results, are absent entirely. The primary score remains 18/24; manual interpretation does not retroactively relax it.

The automated supplemental context checker qualifies 18 links and flags the remaining three because their page scope cannot be resolved under the same strict locator assumption; its fixed context-date field also does not interpret the final context's `openingText` key. Manual source reconciliation establishes correct raw person/date/value links for all 21 returned records, including the page-31/page-37 context. All 90 string-valued payload fields across 53 entries (32 contexts and 21 records) match normalized original source text. Review of all 21 publication summaries, clinical/report fields, coverage notes and retained negations found no additional unsupported critical diagnosis, order, medication or contradictory value/unit/date claim. There are no no-tool prose completions. These observations do not repair missing output or the primary provenance failures.

**Association remains 0/24:** the frozen association criterion requires host `evidencedIdentity.fullName` and `birthDate` matching the fixture. The returned host identity objects retain a fingerprint with confirmation required, rather than those two fields. Correct names/DOBs in raw context therefore do not satisfy that endpoint. This is failed measured association/identity establishment, not demonstrated wrong-person transcription. First and half identity-established/selectable readiness were never reached.

There are **82 HTTP-200 responses**, comprising 80 single-tool responses and two four-tool responses: **88 tool calls = two plan reads + 65 page reads + 21 publications**. Fourteen publications contain context only and seven contain three clinical records. Two model contexts correspond to two budgeted slices. The automatic productive continuation occurs after request 63, with 18 ready records and 34 distinct acknowledged pages. No explicit extra Resume occurred.

**Read counters require three separate counts:** durable attribution records **65 host reads**, while the harness contains **64 successfully returned read receipts**, and durable acknowledgement totals **63**. All 40 distinct pages were ultimately acknowledged. Thus there are 25 host rereads, 24 duplicate returned receipts and 23 duplicate acknowledgements. Page 35 was read before the slice boundary without acknowledgement and then reread; the final page-38 read succeeded on the host but the progress guard rejected its result before exposure. Calling all 64 returned receipts “acknowledged” is inaccurate. Final state has zero pending windows but still zero accounted units out of two; every batch reports `inspected`, not completed extraction coverage.

The final encounter cycles pages 38–40 three times, then requests page 38 again. Request 76 contains exact pages 39/40 but an evicted page-38 receipt; subsequent exact page pairs rotate as in the earlier mixed0 failure. This supports the same context-retention hypothesis for the unproduced final encounter, without proving an intervention would fix it. Earlier sections did produce results, so the outcome cannot be summarized as universal inability to combine distant pages.

Usage rederives to **6,293,278 input + 23,589 output = 6,316,867 tokens**, including **763,392 cached input**, below the unchanged 6,935,932 threshold. All input/output/cache entries are reported. First literal discovery is **78,125.515416 ms**, half expected literal coverage **598,736.894916 ms**, and time to unsuccessful stop **1,084,518.118833 ms**. No budget reset or unresolved usage is involved.

External portable evidence under `mixed1-u20-terminal/` includes the pinned manifest, independent collector, actual-state `verify.mts`/`verification.json`, and separate `manual-audit.json`. Reproduction compares durable state, joins all response tools to execution, sums raw usage, reconciles host/returned/acknowledged reads, decodes source text and applies both the unchanged primary criterion and separately labeled manual grounding checks. No verifier provider call, runtime mutation or Git write occurred. Overall execution completeness remains pending the remaining F2 cells.

## Owner-directed time-bounded F2 closeout — 2026-09-25

Owner's latest instruction says the 120-run campaign taking days is too slow and requests a faster, less redundant way to reach useful conclusions. Preserve the original120-cell plan, thresholds, source files and every attempted outcome; this is an explicit post-results scope reduction, not completion or a passing reinterpretation of that protocol.

Revised deliverable: a bounded decision report from the already attempted prefix, the current mixed1/r1/U2 cell allowed to reach its unchanged terminal guard, and the six fully executed independently verified offline follow-ups. No further live cells start. Explain whether existing evidence supports changing unit size now, observed quality failures, and unresolved claims. Shortened execution cannot earn original noninferiority/confidence or long-report qualification.

Required work: independently verify every terminal prefix result and preserved U20 supplement; preserve unsuccessful outcomes and missing usage. Reconcile all known prefix request/input/output/cache counts without double-counting original U20 usage; identify unknowns and keep any estimated cost separate. Document actualhost/returned/acknowledged read counters, locator-format versus fact errors, and hostidentity versus rawsource association. Publish descriptive comparisons only for complete matched observations; failed-stop durations never count as faster processing.

Use existing independently verified F1 200/800-page scripted results to discuss structural request/transcript growth and the difference between plan-unit size, read size, publication batching, and retained evidence. Label extrapolation as model, not live time or quality. Do not assume size independence or manufacture missing long-report results. Further repetitions of same sixfiles estimate variability, not added document diversity; dropping planned repeats relinquishes their variability estimates.

Do not run original all120-gated finalization: its prerequisite will intentionally remain unmet. Label finalization deferred with original study, not passed. A separately declared future retention-remedy or long-report study may be recommended without turning it into newly required work for this bounded closeout. No product adoption recommendation while current quality requirements fail.

Closeout requires an independent reviewer to compare originalprompt, latesttimeconstraint, all recorded results and revised checklist; give an honest positive/negative judgment of revised execution and useful extension, explicitly recording that original120 execution remains incomplete. Resolve material factual/accounting findings first. Then pause follow-up automation.

Boundary pause uses independently reviewed external marker latch before next untouched cell; preserve original live source and current inference, distinguish ownerpause from a failed/unattempted experiment, retain latchreceipt and afterexit evidence, restore nextcell not_started by archival rename.

**Positive scope review: 9/10; no material blocker. This is approval of the revised deliverable's design, not a final execution score.** I reviewed the exact `f2-time-bounded-closeout-proposal.md` (SHA-256 `42e348f584f50fb3dc9b0fb5c4e48797cb284d6ef2550027a13596531f4f25b4`) against the original request for useful extended experiments and the owner's new constraint that a days-long 120-run campaign is too slow.

The proposal appropriately stops further live dispatch after the active cell, preserves the original protocol and all failures, requires independent verification and complete known-cost reconciliation, and limits conclusions to the evidence actually obtained. Its explicit deferrals of the 120-cell matrix, planned variability/confidence/noninferiority claims, live 200-page single-report behavior and all-120-gated finalization prevent a shortened study from being presented as originally complete. Existing F1 long-workload scripted results can explain modeled structural costs but cannot substitute for those deferred live quality/time results.

The current evidence is sufficient for a bounded decision **not to adopt a universal larger unit size now**: dense live behavior shows a token/time tradeoff, mixed inputs expose substantial progress and association failures, and retained-input scope is distinct from plan-unit size. Further same-file repetitions may estimate variability but are not required to conclude that these observed adoption gates fail. No additional long-report live run is required for this narrower no-adoption closeout; its absence must remain prominent.

One interpretation to retain: descriptive failure patterns and failed quality counts remain valid evidence even where matched successful-job comparisons are unavailable. Fully measured successful-job cost/latency comparisons must stay separate from failed-stop durations, partial jobs and the unknown-cost U20 supplement. The proposal already supports this distinction; it requires no substantive revision.

A final positive revised-execution review remains conditional on verification of the active mixed1 U2 terminal result, the safe boundary stop and latch archival, the complete prefix accounting without duplicated U20 cost, accurate host/returned/acknowledged counts and a final written synthesis that preserves all unmet claims. It must be labeled completion of the owner-revised bounded study, with original 120-run execution explicitly incomplete. The automation should be paused only after this closeout is actually complete. No additional experiment, provider call, runtime mutation or Git write was performed for this review.

## Owner-directed extrapolation checks — declared before supplemental execution

The owner clarified that a full stop was not desired: run a small number of gap-filling tests, then explicitly label defensible projections for unexecuted cases. The owner selected about90minutes of additional live testing. This supersedes the preceding no-more-live closeout while preserving that decision and review as historical entries. The original120-cell matrix remains stopped after14primary outcomes; its unattempted cells will not be relabeled measured or complete.

The supplemental design is three U10 cells in fixed order: a20-page dense chart with a30-minute dispatch budget, a20-page single report with15minutes, and an80-page single report with45minutes. The dense20 sample checks a prospective size prediction from the existing dense40 successes; this is local downward validation. The single20 sample supplies a calibration anchor for a predeclared formula, whose80-page prediction must be frozen before that validation run. The80-page outcome must remain held out from model selection. Three attempted tests are required unless a route/accounting/state failure blocks later dispatch; an earlier poor quality result does not authorize selecting a replacement.

The dense20 fixture preserves the original dense0 first20 clinical pages and80 expected observations, apart from the total-page header. Both single-report lengths preserve the original single0 report title, identity, date and six literal findings. Fact pages are mapped by1+round((originalPage−1)×(N−1)/199):20-page sites2,5,9,12,16,20;80-page sites5,16,32,48,64,80. Identity remains onpage1 and the corrected final finding remains on the last page. Changing length changes evidence spacing; it is not an isolated page-count causal manipulation. These are post-results supplemental variants, not new independent held-out documents for the original confidence analysis.

The same structural token model and2×token stops give modeled totals/stop thresholds of1,254,040/2,508,080 for dense20,1,228,049/2,456,098 for single20, and8,100,367/16,200,734 for single80. These are modeled dispatch guards, not measured consumption or empirical extrapolation accuracy. Wall limits stop dispatch before another provider request, allow an already started response to finish and retain its cost;90minutes is therefore an allocated dispatch budget with possible in-flight grace, not a hard elapsed deadline. Existing application guards, unknown-usage stops and original evidence preservation remain. No extra Resume resets either budget.

The separate input plan binds source and PDF/oracle hashes. Dense source SHA-2564cd5cc5df6046dbff6cc009b06d696ccae5186cf8ed16bdc38788c098f59225b; single20 0845e9d238aaccd9d04a4e690e589a84797614d7ffd738bb20e67ab107c0599a; single80 ffa9bfdf693ea4c00cb5ea441c8b6cd5125376315f1df5b642f54a33bdb4ea1b. These are author checks, not independent pre-use clearance. Final runner/controller/prediction hashes and independent review are still required before live dispatch.

The resulting register will distinguish measured outcomes, conditional EXTRAPOLATED numerical scenarios and NOT_ESTIMABLE endpoints. ## F2 prospective extrapolation models and independent supplemental clearance

### Prospective scaling models and missing-cell register — frozen before supplemental live calls

The owner narrowed the unfinished 120-run study to a bounded gap-fill and explicit modeling exercise. The original 14 measured primary attempts, the separate U20 continuation, all failures, and every unattempted cell remain distinct. No modeled row becomes a completed experiment, and no modeled quality result enters a quality gate or pooled efficacy statistic.

**Scripted mechanism.** For the fixed F1 schedule, let `C = N + ceil(N/U) + 2`: one operation per page, one publication per unit, and two plan operations. Each 64-round session has at most 63 executable operations plus a final acknowledgment, so `S = ceil(C/63)` and `R = C + S`. This reproduces the recorded request and session counts in all 14 original matrix cells and both tail checks, including the explicitly resumed N800/U2 run. It is conditional on that scripted schedule; the actual live model chooses its own reads, publications and context restarts.

The corresponding text volume is not exactly proportional to length. Multiplying the N200 character count by four underestimates the observed N800 count by 9.18%–27.97% across the seven unit sizes, using `(prediction − actual) / actual`. The observed text ratios are 4.404–5.553 for four times as many pages. The report preserves a simple proportional scenario and a two-anchor power curve `T(N,U)=T(200,U)*(N/200)^e(U)`, where `e(U)=log(T(800,U)/T(200,U))/log(4)`. The latter fits the two endpoints by construction; it initially has no independent intermediate-length validation. The generated grid covers N40/80/100/200/400/800 and U2/10/15/20. Measured endpoints, interpolation at N400 and downward extrapolation are explicitly identified. Neither curve estimates provider tokens or quality.

**Live dense-family scenario.** Calibration uses only six strict-complete, fully accounted dense40 primary attempts: both U10 and U15 documents, and dense1 at U2 and U20. Dense0/U2 remains incomplete despite full literal coverage; dense0/U20 has an unknown request charge and is excluded. For each available same-U anchor, `M(N)=B+(N/40)*(M(40)-B)` for input tokens, output tokens, requests and elapsed-time scenario. `B` is the contribution of the first two attempted requests, treated as an assumed fixed allowance; it can include a page read and is not pure measured setup overhead. Results average the available same-U anchors. Individual-anchor spread is descriptive, not a confidence interval, repeated-run variance or prediction interval. Fractional requests are scenario means, never executed counts.

The dense20/U10 prediction, frozen before its execution, is **1,566,967.5 input + 34,641 output = 1,601,608.5 tokens**, **60.25 requests**, and an exploratory elapsed-time scenario of **1,245,534.007 ms (20.7589 minutes)**. The two individual-anchor predictions span 1,588,979–1,614,238 tokens, 58.5–62 requests and 20.2071–21.3107 minutes. These narrow spans do not establish narrow uncertainty. Cache counts already belong to input usage and are not added again. No currency costs are computed.

**Prospective single-family model.** The 20- and 80-page supplemental single reports each contain six findings. Before either runs, the primary output rule is therefore fixed as: first-two-request output + later publication-only response output + `(N/N0)` times remaining response output. Publication output is assumed fixed for equal fact count; publication batching and wording can still change. Input/request/time scenarios retain the affine rule above. The earlier scalar-output model is preserved as a secondary baseline. Both predictions remain conditional; no extraction, identity, association, failure-probability or cache forecast is made. No-tool summaries may contain clinical narrative and remain in the scaled “other” bucket, a limitation of this simple decomposition.

After single20 terminates, the controller must write an exclusive, immutable `single80-prediction.json` before dispatching single80. The helper binds the model source, plan object, calibration result object and each raw response hash. It reconciles response usage to the request ledger and partitions first-two/publication-only/other responses without overlap. Incomplete or incompletely accounted calibration writes `NOT_ESTIMABLE` with null values. Missing/malformed response structure or mixed publication/other calls makes primary output and total unsupported while retaining the input/request/time scenarios and scalar baseline separately. A failed calibration does not turn a later test into a predicted success.

The prospective sensitivity criterion is `abs(actual − predicted) / predicted ≤ 25%` for input, output, total tokens and requests; elapsed time has an exploratory 35% criterion. These are model-error checks, not confidence intervals or adoption gates. Compute full-job error only for complete, fully accounted outcomes. Preserve censored/failed attempts and their known partial costs separately. No formula or primary selection will be tuned to the 80-page outcome.

**120-cell register.** The first offline register contains 14 `MEASURED` primary attempts, 32 unexecuted dense-family `EXTRAPOLATED` conditional cost scenarios, and 74 rows whose empirical completion forecast is `NOT_ESTIMABLE`. All 120 retain their original frozen `modeledTokens` structural estimate as a distinct field; an unavailable empirical forecast does not erase that older, unvalidated estimate. Every unexecuted repeat uses the same deterministic scenario, with no invented repeat variability. Mixed-family failures cannot calibrate successful completion. The original 200-page single-report cells initially lack a successful family anchor. Unexecuted quality and reliable latency remain `NOT_ESTIMABLE` in every family. Supplemental anchors and any later model-error findings are separate additions, not replacements for this original register.

**Artifacts and reproduction.** The external evidence bundle contains `predict.ts`, `predict.test.ts`, `build.ts`, `initial-model-freeze.json`, all preserved revision sources, `final-model-freeze-v4.json`, `model-report.json`, `original-120-register.json`, and `scripted-length-grid.csv`. Inputs are the original F1 `summary.json`, frozen F2 `run-plan.json`, and each original primary `result.json`/`response-N.json`; exact input hashes are recorded in the initial freeze. The final prediction module SHA-256 is `67d206dce829585b29805120ef90f04acb57e55cde4220a7743981a7503e3d9f`; the final rule/dense20 artifact SHA-256 is `b6dc3f8d48676a83cfcf5c4b0c3dbe782bcfef83c1730de43c908c9b3c634fc6`. Earlier freezes and prediction files were preserved through pre-use numeric/schema validation improvements; numerical dense20 predictions were unchanged. `node --test MODEL/predict.test.ts` passes six focused cases, and standalone TypeScript compilation with ES2023/ESNext/bundler resolution passes. Independent review is recorded separately; these are author checks, not self-verification.

### F8 frozen conditional execution declaration and independent clearance

### F8 — preregistered exact-source retention remedy, offline runner entry (2026-09-26)

This is a new isolated experiment, with the original failed and partial recordings preserved. The hypothesis is that removing exact source text from the model's context contributes to repeated reading and incomplete report assembly. A controlled comparison confirms exposure loss: under the prior compaction policy, page 1 disappears from a later request; under the prototype, the identical source text remains. This does not establish that retention alone fixes clinical report quality.

The opt-in prototype retains literal source text in the current bridge, pins one copy of each byte-distinct instruction block, and replays only successful reads subsequently acknowledged by a valid model response into later bridge contexts. The host ledger binds the profile, intake and source hash, rejects a changed source, deep-clones metadata, excludes failed/pending reads and preserves historical version labels. Replayed content is marked as source data. The existing repetition guard, page units (10, no overlap), model route, source and acceptance contracts, and context envelope remain. No source fact, oracle or unread page is preloaded. Context size is checked after replay, with refusal rather than truncation. Native-PDF evidence is tested; scanned/OCR-only behavior is not established.

Offline commands, executed from the isolated prototype checkout:

```sh
node --test src/scripts/experiment-rereading-*.test.ts src/server/test/proxy-model-bridge.test.ts
npm run typecheck
npm run format:check
node src/scripts/experiment-rereading-control.ts declare
node src/scripts/experiment-rereading-control.ts preflight
```

The focused suite passed **101/101 tests** (including unchanged/default bridge behavior). All four typecheck targets and formatting passed. A real bridge test spans 82 mocked requests, 80 acknowledged pages and two contexts, retaining all 80 exact source texts and six distributed markers. A second test executes the actual runtime, intake, batch, native-PDF reading and ledger: 23 mocked requests read all 20 fictional pages across two contexts, and the final replay contains all six source facts. It makes no clinical proposals and deliberately scores zero clinical correctness. These are transport/memory proofs, not simulated successful model outcomes. The first frozen mechanism proof was recorded at 04:53:50 UTC, before the 05:44:23 diagnostic checkpoint. CLI preflight made zero provider calls and opened no runtime.

### F8 terminal outcome — reading recovered, fixed report gate failed

### F8 — live remedy regression and conditional stop (2026-09-26)

The exact-source retention prototype read **all 20 pages once**, completed its reading workflow, and preserved all six fictional findings. It **did not assemble the required complete report**. The declared prerequisite therefore failed, and the dependent 40- and 80-page arms were correctly left **unattempted / prerequisite_failed**. Scaling predictions and the 200-page extension are **NOT_ESTIMABLE**. No failed outcome was retried or relabeled successful.

The sole command was `node src/scripts/experiment-rereading-control.ts run`, after independent pre-use clearance of declaration `53b8a5de82d023b190b36f8e6babfaf7dff7748916b2922abde21aabd518c539`. It began at 05:11:21.725 UTC and recorded the conditional stop at 05:16:13.752 UTC. The 20-page runtime lasted **291,929.143 ms (4.866 minutes)**. All 24 physical provider attempts returned HTTP 200 with known usage: **967,766 input + 6,671 output = 974,437 tokens**, including 211,200 cached input tokens. There were no new unknown attempts, route errors, token-cap crossings, deadline interruptions or explicit Resume extensions. The controller exited zero, released its lock, and the isolated proxy was stopped. All app containers remained stopped.

Both successful delivered reads and the host attribution subsequently reviewed independently cover pages 1 through 20 once, with no rereads. Unlike the earlier 20-page run, which cycled through pages 1/2/3 and stopped at `no_progress`, this run reached `reading_exhausted` with no remaining reading units. This single historical-fixture comparison supports the retention hypothesis as a remedy for the observed loop; it is not a randomized comparison, proof of universal causality or repeatability result. The live run used one bridge context; cross-context retention was demonstrated offline, not live at 80 pages.

The strict original document endpoint remains **0/1 correct**, **0/6 facts inside the expected full document**, **0/6 machine-qualified finding-page links**, and 0 correct associations. Seven review records were returned: a document containing the first-page header, plus six separate observation records carrying the six literal findings. The six observations are unexpected under this one-document oracle; this label does **not** mean their source text was invented. Two proposal publications retained additional literal pages in context envelopes. All seven records remained identity-pending and unselectable; no clinical records were accepted. Workflow completion alone is therefore not successful import review or a complete report.

The provenance limitation is narrower than “no source link”: each returned record's source URL points to the retained original, and its prose locator includes the applicable finding page and page 1 for identity/date. The document locator mentions pages 1 and 20. The frozen `exactQualificationPage` parser rejects a locator with multiple distinct page numbers, so these human-resolvable links do not meet its single-page qualification. A read-only offline parser check on cloned metadata confirms that separating the page references yields individual page matches; it does not alter the recorded grade or fix the absent full-document payload. All six literal facts survive in the separate observation payloads, while none appears in the document's own payload.

This failure identifies two issues beyond memory: the desired full-document representation is not guaranteed by the current extraction contract, and finding-versus-context provenance needs distinct machine-readable links. Existing model instructions allow component records linked to context and instruct the model to keep subject unknown until explicitly tied to the selected person. Retaining source text does not change those contracts. These are diagnostic findings for a separately designed future remedy, not an unplanned implementation or live retry. A useful subsequent study would predeclare whether a complete report plus component findings is acceptable, test explicit report assembly without dropping literal evidence, and test separate finding/context page references while preserving identity review. The present criteria remain unchanged.

F8 adds only the 24 real provider attempts above. Mocked offline requests are excluded. The **seven earlier unknown-usage attempts remain unknown**; these are cumulative known lower bounds, not a monetary invoice or complete consumption estimate. Result SHA-256: `8469a4bc6013710494673637c5b367445b9273ab1cbf7c1efca56093a61efe7a`.

### F8 contract reconciliation and separate F9 decision

### F8 post hoc contract reconciliation — what actually remains incomplete

**The retained source information is substantially more complete than the failed full-document score suggests.** For this fictional native-text PDF, F8 retained the complete literal text of all twenty pages across two context envelopes and all six findings in separate clinical observation envelopes, linked into one coherent host report group. The recorded F8 gate still fails: there is no single clinical document payload containing the whole report, the single-page qualification helper rejects composite locators, and identity remains unconfirmed. These are different statements; none should replace another.

This is an offline, post hoc reconciliation of unchanged artifacts. It makes no new provider calls, changes no frozen criterion, and does not relabel F8 as a preregistered success.

**Why the brief and normal application contract differ.** The processing proposal explicitly modeled “single record” as one logical report spanning the document, with one small output record (`docs/todo/CRS-086.md:311–328`). F2 then implemented the single-report oracle as one document containing six cross-page facts; F8 explicitly retained that prerequisite (`docs/experiments/processing-follow-up-experiments.md`, F2 and F8 conditional-arm clarification). That is a legitimate experimental endpoint, but it is narrower than the current application's extraction contract.

The maintained assistant instructions permit component rows linked to one retained context, discourage duplicating shared headers into every payload, and require the same evidenced report/subject anchor across later pages (`src/server/assistant-instructions.md:250–279`). They explicitly distinguish complete scoped context from selective clinical envelopes: complete literal page/section text can live in a context envelope while component excerpts remain partial, and retained evidence need not project every fact into clinical tables (`src/server/assistant-instructions.md:289–305`). `src/server/clinical-instructions.ts` defines one clinical entry per envelope and allows separately sourced observations. These instructions do not require every coherent report to be reassembled into one clinical document payload.

The host implements this separation rather than merely trusting a label. `resolveReportContexts` requires one matching same-proposal context alias, compatible source system/report claims, retained literal report/subject anchors and matching package scope (`src/server/intake-report-context.ts:130–223`). `recordReportGroups` scopes a group by original ID/hash, source system, member, actual report/subject anchors and clinical subject, then carries prior members forward into immutable group versions (`src/server/intake-report-groups.ts:45–179`). Context linkage and grouping are explicitly unreviewed source claims, not patient confirmation (`src/shared/intake.ts:42–73`).

**What the actual retained artifacts show.** The two proposals contain context pages 1–10 and pages 1 plus 11–20. Their seven clinical records are one header-only document plus six finding observations. The host records one `report_anchor` group with a four-member first version and seven-member final version, both with resolved uniform context links. It does not create seven unrelated report groups. The final version points to the second scoped context, while earlier proposal/context evidence remains durable; no aggregate all-pages clinical document is manufactured. The independent terminal audit matched all twenty-eight emitted page/text segments to the actual PDF and all six clinical findings to their printed source. Thus, for this controlled text-only source, there is no demonstrated loss of page text or a clinical fact from retained evidence. That conclusion does not establish an equally complete current UI presentation, image/annotation fidelity, every arbitrary source format, or production readiness.

**What is a real remaining gap, and what is safe abstention?**

- The expected full-document representation is missing. If the product truly needs a single complete document object in addition to component findings, that is a separate assembly requirement to design and test; literal retention alone does not provide it.
- Every record links to the correct original, and a person can resolve the cited finding and identity pages. The fixed `exactQualificationPage` helper (`src/scripts/provider-qualification-fixture.ts:409–445`) accepts exactly one distinct page, so “page 2, finding; identity on page 1” deliberately fails. This is a machine-qualification/schema mismatch, not absent original provenance. The smallest representation improvement would store separate finding-page and context-page references, with source binding and role kept explicit. Merely making the existing helper choose the first number would weaken its ambiguity guard and is not recommended.
- All seven candidates require identity confirmation. This is safe review behavior, not evidence that their literal information was lost or assigned to the wrong person. The instructions forbid using `subject:self` as a completeness marker, and host identity policy keeps printed but insufficiently established identity pending (`src/server/assistant-instructions.md:250–258`; `src/server/intake-identity-policy.ts:523–579`). A new extraction-quality endpoint could allow safe unknown identity while rejecting wrong-person assignment, but it must continue to report identity readiness separately and permit no acceptance.
- The prior-comparison measurement lacks its historical date; retaining unknown with a review issue is supported abstention. Copying the encounter date into it to satisfy a simplistic date check would invent precision.

**Actionable choice.** Do not spend additional calls forcing the model to duplicate a complete source report into one clinical document solely to satisfy the original cost model's shape. The measured useful outcome is stronger information retention and successful traversal, with unresolved representation/qualification requirements. Closing the investigation here is honest if the decision is whether retention is worth further product work; it remains unqualified for adoption or long-report scaling.

If the owner still wants a short scaling experiment within the remaining shared window, a defensible next study would use a **new, prospectively declared source-complete coherent-group endpoint**, with the original full-document endpoint still reported as a secondary unchanged failure metric. This endpoint is exploratory and post hoc for the existing F8 twenty-page recording, not a replacement pass. Declare the new study before observing any further outcomes:

1. Require all pages' literal text retained as exactly source-bound, explicitly scoped context/record evidence; all six authored findings retained without critical additions or changes; same original/report/printed-person grouping; all source segments independently reconciled; no cross-report or wrong-person attribution; unchanged reading and context caps; fully known usage. Safe unknown identity and properly qualified partial component envelopes are allowed, with identity readiness and single-page machine qualification reported separately.
2. Independently test that the evaluator rejects a missing late fact/page, wrong original, conflicting report/subject, altered negation/value/unit, unjustified complete-coverage claim and an unresolvable context link. Do not teach the model fixture facts or alter its prompt to match an oracle. A human source audit remains mandatory.
3. Use the previously unattempted forty-page fixture once as a new calibration, and the distinct eighty-page fixture once as held-out validation only if the forty-page source-complete gate passes. Retain the twenty-page F8 numbers only as an explicitly exploratory calibration anchor for this new endpoint; no claim that F8 preregistered quality passed. Freeze the same power/linear prediction method now, and the numerical eighty-page prediction after forty but before eighty. No claim of clinical-quality extrapolation. One eighty-page run would also exercise the actual context-turnover path that F8 proved only offline.
4. Preserve original F8 grades and all costs, allow no unknown-usage waiver or retry, and keep the absolute 07:44:23 last-dispatch / 08:44:23 hard-finish deadlines. If the evaluator cannot be independently qualified in time, or the new prerequisite fails, stop with NOT_ESTIMABLE.

That proposed study would test a different, application-supported question—whether complete source information remains coherently reviewable as document length grows. It would not prove a complete clinical-document representation, automatic patient association or acceptance, and requires an explicit coordinator decision and independent pre-use review before execution. No new live work is authorized or started by this reconciliation.

### Independent post hoc contract reconciliation and next-study recommendation — 2026-09-26

**Recommendation: a separately declared, prospectively gated 40→80-page study is useful, provided it evaluates source-complete coherent report evidence rather than relabeling F8.** The existing F8 result remains a failure under its frozen complete-document endpoint. The normal application contract supports a different endpoint: one source-bound report group, literal scoped context, independently sourced clinical components, and explicit human identity review. These are different questions, not interchangeable grades.

I independently read the proposal's single-record definition (`docs/todo/CRS-086.md:309–328`), extraction instructions (`src/server/assistant-instructions.md:250–305`), clinical mapping instructions, report-context resolver, report-group recording, report queue projection and maintained intake contract. “Single record” in the cost proposal explicitly models one logical report and one small output object. Its one-document experimental oracle is a legitimate narrower requirement. The extraction instructions instead expressly allow linked component rows, require component-local evidence, discourage repeating shared headers, permit complete literal scoped context alongside partial clinical envelopes, and keep identity unknown until tied to the selected person. Thus the six supported observations are not intrinsically erroneous or hallucinated because the full-document oracle expects one row.

**Independent runtime check.** I queried the stopped fictional runtime through the actual read-only report-queue APIs. It presents one `report_anchor` group, seven records across two exact proposal blocks (four plus three), and two uniform-context versions growing from four to seven members. The two context versions retain distinct envelope references; they are not an automatically assembled all-pages clinical document. The prior independent source audit established all twenty pages' exact text across the retained context envelopes and all six authored findings in the observations. All original and context references remain available through the exact proposal blocks. I did not test browser presentation or navigation, so “source evidence retained and exposed through the review APIs” is supported; “every relevant page is convenient to review in the UI” is not established.

The host accurately reports `allSourceOccurrencesAccounted: true`, `clinicalExtraction: unknown`, `extractionComplete: false`, and `allCurrentReportsReviewed: false`. The maintained intake contract deliberately never infers complete clinical extraction merely from a complete reading plan. No new evaluator may silently turn these host states into accepted or fully reviewed clinical data.

**Quality distinctions to preserve.** All seven candidates remain identity-confirmation-required; this is safe abstention and does not alone demonstrate wrong-person attribution. Every critical mapped fact still needs a literal and clinically correct association to the original report/printed person. The prior comparison's historical date must remain unknown absent a printed date. Original-source URLs are correct, while composite “finding page X; identity page 1” locators fail the unchanged single-page qualification helper. A new evidence audit may independently resolve explicitly role-labeled finding and identity locators without claiming the existing helper passed; choosing the first number indiscriminately would weaken provenance. Full-document assembly, precise machine-readable provenance, selected-person confirmation, clinical acceptance and browser usability remain separate unmet milestones.

**Minimum conditions for a useful new study.** Freeze a new F9 declaration before any additional inference. Keep F8 and its grades unchanged. Treat F8's twenty-page observation as a post hoc exploratory calibration anchor only; declare forty pages as the first new prospective gate and eighty pages as held-out validation. Use the same unchanged fictional fixtures, source retention mechanism, ordinary progress/reading limits, scope checks and accounting. Do not teach fixture answers to the model or force a particular number of envelopes merely to reproduce the observed seven.

The new criterion should require every original page's literal text retained in source-bound, accurately scoped proposal evidence; all six authored facts retained with correct values, units, negation, temporal role and source associations; one evidenced report/printed-subject grouping; no unsupported critical facts, cross-report/person attribution, duplicate clinical assertions, conflicting complete-coverage claims or dangling context links. It must independently audit every submitted clinical envelope and source segment, not only search for the six answer strings. Safe unknown identity and partial component envelopes can be allowed, with zero accepted writes. Host group labels alone are not proof: wrong original/report/person and missing/ambiguous context must fail the evaluator. Schema qualification, identity readiness and the original full-document grade should remain separate reported outcomes.

Before live use, negative controls should remove a late fact or source page, change an original or report/person binding, alter a value/unit/negation/temporal role, introduce an unsupported or duplicate clinical assertion, overclaim complete coverage, and break a context reference. The evaluator must reject each. At least one positive fixture should use a valid alternative component representation so that the criterion is not just fitted to F8's exact observed output shape. Independent manual critical-claim and accounting review must gate the next arm even after automated checks pass.

Freeze the two-point power model and linear baseline before observing forty pages; bind the resulting numeric eighty-page prediction before eighty dispatch. F8's post hoc twenty-page anchor makes this an exploratory resource-scaling study, not a preregistered confirmation of a universal law. Eighty pages is especially useful because it can exercise actual context turnover, which F8 only exercised offline. If eighty finishes within one context, report that limitation instead of claiming live turnover coverage. Any 200-page projection is conditional and cannot forecast quality or completion. One forty/eighty run each cannot estimate natural run-to-run variance.

Retain the original shared four-hour clock, last-dispatch and hard-finish deadlines, cumulative token caps and every earlier cost/unknown. No retry or unknown-usage waiver follows from this recommendation. A failed new prerequisite remains a stopped new study with unattempted dependent arms, not permission to refit or keep testing until a pass. If a qualified evaluator and independent pre-use review cannot fit comfortably inside the remaining window, close with the present useful mechanistic result and an explicitly unanswered scaling question.

This review recommends a new question on documented product behavior. It is neither authorization for live work nor a revision of F8. I made no provider/authentication calls, changed no runtime or production source, and used read-only Git. Reproducible additional command: `node contract-queue-audit.mts` against the retained stopped fictional runtime; its output records the one-group/seven-record/two-block structure and conservative host activity state. The attempted optional `--import tsx` invocation failed before loading the audit; native Node then ran it successfully with no dependency changes.
